\begin{equation}\label{stratification delta}
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}=\bigsqcup_{\delta\in\mathbb{N}}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_\delta
\end{equation}
called \emph{the stratification with constant $\delta$}.

Consider the big stratum $\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{\psi_G}$ for the maximal element $\psi_G$ of $\Psi$. This stratum consists of points $a\in\mathcal{A}(\bar{k})$ such that the cameral curve $\widetilde X_a$ is smooth. In this case the invariant $\delta$ equals $0$. In particular, the stratum $\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{0}$ in the $\delta$-constant stratification above is a nonempty open subset of $\mathcal{A}$. In general, it strictly contains the open set $\mathcal{A}_{\psi_G}$, as shown by the following example.

In the case $G=\operatorname{GL}_r$, the description of $\mathcal{P}_a$ in terms of the spectral curve implies that $\delta_a=0$ if and only if the spectral curve $Y_a$ is smooth. It may happen that the spectral curve is smooth with a branch point $y\in Y_a$ of order greater than $2$. In that case the cameral curve $\widetilde X_a$ is not smooth. Thus, in the $\operatorname{GL}_r$ case the stratification by family normalization of cameral curves strictly refines the stratification by family normalization of spectral curves.

% [journal 5.6.6] MISSING FROM THE arXiv VERSION. This is the stratification that
% 7.8.1 refers to; without it the reference there is to the A^heartsuit stratification.
\begin{numero}\label{stratification delta ani}
One deduces from this a stratification of the open subset $\tilde\calA^\ani$
$$\tilde\calA^\ani=\bigsqcup_{\delta\in\NN}\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta.$$
Note that for every $\tilde a=(a,\tilde\infty)\in\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta(\bar k)$, by \ref{dimension Bezru globale}, the dimension of the affine part of $\calP^0_a$ equals $\delta$.
\end{numero}

\subsection{Stratification by Radicial Valuations}
\label{subsection : valuations radicielles}

Let $\bar{v}$ be a geometric point of $\bar X$ and denote by $\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}}$ the completion of $\bar X$ at $\bar{v}$ and by $\bar F_{\bar{v}}$ its field of fractions. By choosing a uniformizer $\varepsilon_{\bar{v}}$, one may identify $\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}}$ with the ring $\bar k[[\varepsilon_{\bar{v}}]]$. We choose a trivialization of the restriction of $\rho_G$ to $\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}}$ that splits $G$ and, in particular, provides an isomorphism $W=\mathbb{W}$.

We now briefly review the analysis of the stratification of $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})$ by radicial valuations as in \cite{GKM-codim}, due to Goresky, Kottwitz, and MacPherson. Their strata of radicial valuations are finer than our strata defined by family normalization and, a fortiori, finer than the strata with constant $\delta$.

Let $a\in\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}})$ and let $J_a=a^*J$ be the smooth group scheme over $\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}}$ associated to $a$. The generic fiber of $J_a$ is a torus whose monodromy can be described using the cameral cover (see \ref{J=J'}). Let $\bar F_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{sep}}$ be the separable closure of $\bar F_{\bar{v}}$. Let $x\in \mathfrak{t}(\bar F_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{sep}})$ be a $\bar F_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{sep}}$-point of $\mathfrak{t}$ mapping to $a\in \mathfrak{c}(\bar F_{\bar{v}})$. The choice of this point defines a homomorphism
$$\pi_a^\bullet: I_{\bar{v}} \longrightarrow \mathbb{W},$$
where $I_{\bar{v}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\bar F_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{sep}}/\bar F_{\bar{v}})$. Since the characteristic of $k$ does not divide the order of $\mathbb{W}$, $\pi_a^\bullet$ factors through the tame quotient $I_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{tame}}$ of $I_{\bar{v}}$. To follow the notations of \cite{GKM-codim}, choose a topological generator of $I_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{tame}}$ and denote by $w_a$ the image of this generator under $\pi_a^\bullet$.

For every root $\alpha\in \Phi$, define an integer
$$r(\alpha):=\operatorname{val}_{\bar{v}}(\alpha(x)),$$
where $\operatorname{val}_{\bar{v}}$ is the unique extension of the valuation with $\operatorname{val}_{\bar{v}}(\varepsilon_{\bar{v}})=1$ on $\bar F_{\bar{v}}$ to $\bar F_{\bar{v}}^{\mathrm{sep}}$. This yields a function 
$$r:\Phi\rightarrow \mathbb{Q}_+.$$

The pair $(w_a,r)$ depends on the choice of $x$, but its $\mathbb{W}$-orbit does not. We denote by $[w_a,r]$ the $\mathbb{W}$-orbit of the pair $(w_a,r)$.

We have the obvious equality
$$\sum_{\alpha\in \Phi} r(\alpha)=\deg_{\bar{v}}(a^*\operatorname{discrim}_G)=d_{\bar{v}}(a).$$
The invariant
$$c_{\bar{v}}(a)=\dim(\mathfrak{t})-\dim(\mathfrak{t}^{w_a})$$
is the drop in the toric rank of the N\'eron model of $J_a$. According to Bezrukavnikov's formula, we have
$$\delta_{\bar{v}}(a)=\frac{d_{\bar{v}}(a)-c_{\bar{v}}(a)}{2}.$$

Let $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})_{[w,r]}$ be the subset of $a\in\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})$ with the invariant $[w,r]$ as above. According to \cite{GKM-codim}, this set is admissible, i.e., there exists an integer $N$ and a locally closed subscheme $Z$ of $\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}}/\varepsilon_{\bar{v}}^N\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})$ viewed as a $\bar{k}$-scheme such that $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})_{[w,r]}$ is the inverse image of $Z(\bar{k})$ under the natural projection 
$$\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})\rightarrow \mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}}/\varepsilon_{\bar{v}}^N\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}}).$$
We say that $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})_{[w,r]}$ is admissible of level $N$.

They then define the \emph{codimension of the stratum} $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})_{[w,r]}$ as the codimension of $Z$ in $\mathfrak{c}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}}/\varepsilon_{\bar{v}}^N\mathcal{O}_{\bar{v}})$ (viewed as a $\bar{k}$-scheme). This codimension clearly does not depend on the level $N$ chosen, provided that $N$ is sufficiently large. We denote this codimension by ${\rm codim}[w,r]$. According to \cite[8.2.2]{GKM-codim} one has the explicit formula
$${\rm codim}[w,r]=d(w,r)+\frac{d_{\bar{v}}(a)+c_{\bar{v}}(a)}{2},$$
where the integer $d(w,r)$ is the codimension of $\mathfrak{t}_w(\mathcal{O})_r$ in $\mathfrak{t}_w(\mathcal{O})$ (in the notations of loc. cit.). We will be content with a coarser estimate.

\begin{proposition}
If $\delta_a>0$, then
$$
{\rm codim}[w,r] \geq \delta_a+1.
$$
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
It is clear that
$$
{\rm codim}[w,r]= \delta_{\bar{v}}(a)+ c_{\bar{v}}(a)+ d(w,r),
$$
where $\delta_{\bar{v}}(a)=\frac{d_{\bar{v}}(a)-c_{\bar{v}}(a)}{2}$. If $w$ is not the trivial element of $W$, then $c_{\bar{v}}(a)\geq 1$. If $w=1$, by the definition in \cite[8.2.2]{GKM-codim}, $d(w,r)$ is the codimension of $\mathfrak{t}(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}})_r$ in $\mathfrak{t}(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}})$, where $\mathfrak{t}(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}})_r$ is the admissible part of $\mathfrak{t}(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{v}})$ consisting of elements having radicial valuation $r$. If $\delta_{\bar{v}}(a) > 0$, then $r\neq 0$ so that this part is of strictly positive codimension. Hence $d(w,r)\geq 1$. In either case, we obtain the desired inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{codimension}
If the degree of $D$ is very large compared to $\delta$, then the $\delta$-constant stratum $\mathcal{A}_\delta$ has codimension at least $\delta$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For any partition $\delta_\bullet$ of $\delta$ as a sum of natural numbers $\delta=\delta_1+\cdots+\delta_n$, consider the subscheme $Z_{\delta_\bullet}$ of $\mathcal{A}^\heartsuit\times X^n$ consisting of tuples $(a;x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ with $a\in\mathcal{A}^\heartsuit(\bar{k})$ and $x_1,\ldots,x_n\in X(\bar{k})$ such that the local invariant $\delta_{x_i}(a)$ equals $\delta_i$. One may stratify $Z_{\delta_\bullet}$ as the union of the strata $Z_{[w_\bullet,r_\bullet]}$ of those $(a;x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ for which the image in $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{x_i})$ lies in the radicial valuation stratum $\mathfrak{c}^\heartsuit(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{x_i})_{[w_i,r_i]}$. Suppose that each such stratum is admissible of level $N_i$. For $\deg(D)$ very large, the linear map
$$\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow \prod_{i=1}^n\mathfrak{c}(\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{x_i}/\varepsilon^{N_i}\bar{\mathcal{O}}_{x_i})$$
is surjective (see \ref{deg(D)>2g}). It follows that $Z_{[w_\bullet,r_\bullet]}$ has codimension at least 
$$\sum_{i=1}^n(\delta_i+1)$$
in $\mathcal{A}\times X^n$. Hence its image in $\mathcal{A}$ has codimension at least $\delta=\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_i$. This proves the proposition.
\end{proof}

We believe that this inequality holds without the hypothesis that $\deg(D)$ is very large relative to $\delta$. A calculation of the infinitesimal action of $\mathcal{P}_a$ on $\mathcal{M}_a$ shows that it is true in characteristic zero. We will present that calculation on another occasion.

%===================================================================================
% [journal chapter 6, 6.1 - 6.4] RE-TRANSLATED FROM THE JOURNAL (pp. 104-110).
% The arXiv version defined A^ani via strata of Psi, stated 6.1.3 without the coarse
% moduli space, carried a "Galois criterion" proposition with no journal counterpart,
% left the proof of 6.3.2 unfinished, and opened 6.4 with material the journal places
% after Theorem 6.4.1. The arXiv text is preserved at ch6/OLD_61_64_arxiv.tex.
%===================================================================================
\section{Cohomology over the Anisotropic Open Set}
\label{section : anisotrope}

In this chapter we shall state the geometric stabilization theorems \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A geom} and \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}. They give an endoscopic description of an isotypic component of the perverse cohomology sheaves of the direct image
$${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)$$
with respect to the action of $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$. Although the statement \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A} implies the fundamental lemma of Langlands and Shelstad \ref{LS}, the proof of \ref{LS} forms one step of the proof of \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}.

\subsection{The Anisotropic Open Set}
\label{subsection : ouvert anisotrope}

By varying the point $\infty$, it follows from \ref{ouvert ani tilde A} and \ref{pi} that there exists an open subset $\calA^\ani$ of $\calA^\heartsuit$ whose points are the $a\in\calA^\ani$ such that the group of connected components $\pi_0(\calP_a)$ is finite. It follows from \ref{borne automorphisme} that for every $a\in\calA^\ani(\bar k)$ and $(E,\phi)\in\calM_a(\bar k)$, the group $\Aut(E,\phi)$ is a finite reduced group, under the hypothesis that the order of $\bbW\rtimes\Theta$ is prime to the characteristic.

\begin{numero}\label{ani auto}
In \cite[II.4]{Fa}, Faltings proved the semi-stable reduction theorem for Higgs bundles, which states that a semi-stable Higgs bundle on $X$ with coefficients in the fraction field of a discrete valuation ring can be extended to a Higgs bundle over the ring after a finite separable extension, and furthermore, if the Higgs bundle in the special fiber is stable, this extension is unique. We refer to \cite{Fa} for the definition of semi-stable and stable Higgs bundles. Let us just say that it is exactly the same definition as for $G$-torsors, except that one considers only the parabolic reductions of $E$ compatible with the Higgs field $\phi$. This suffices to prove the following lemma.
\end{numero}

\begin{lemme}\label{ani > stable}
Let $a\in\calA^\ani(\bar k)$ and $(E,\varphi)\in\calM^\ani(\bar k)$. Then $(E,\phi)$ is stable.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
Since $a\in\calA^\ani(\bar k)$, $E$ has no parabolic reduction compatible with $\phi$.
\end{proof}

We may now apply Faltings' results in \cite{Fa} to obtain the following statement.

\begin{proposition}\label{propre}
The restriction $\calP^\ani$ of the Picard stack $\calP$ to $\calA^\ani$ is a separated smooth Deligne-Mumford stack of finite type over $\calA^\ani$. The open subset $\calM^\ani:=\calM\times_\calA\calA^\ani$ of $\calM$ is a separated smooth Deligne-Mumford stack of finite type over $k$.

Moreover, there exists a coarse moduli space $M^\ani$ such that the morphism $f^\ani:\calM^\ani\rightarrow\calA^\ani$ factors as a homeomorphism $\calM^\ani\rightarrow M^\ani$ followed by a projective morphism $M^\ani\rightarrow\calA^\ani$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We already know that $\calP$ is a smooth Picard stack over $\calA^\heartsuit$ \cf \ref{P lisse} and that $\calM$ is smooth over $k$ \cf \ref{lisse}.

By \cite[II.4]{Fa} and \ref{ani > stable}, $\calM^\ani$ is separated; in other words the diagonal morphism of $\calM^\ani$ is universally closed. We know that it is quasi-finite with reduced fibers. We deduce that it is finite and unramified. Consequently $\calM^\ani$ is a separated Deligne-Mumford stack. The same holds for $\calP^\ani$, since $\calP^\ani$ is identified with an open subset of $\calM^\ani$.

It remains to show that $\calP$ is of finite type and that $\calM$ is proper over $\calA^\ani$. Having the valuative criterion of properness at our disposal \cf \cite[II.4]{Fa}, it suffices to show that it is of finite type over $\calA^\ani$. We know that $\calM$ is locally of finite type. For every $a\in\calA^\ani(\bar k)$ one may choose a family of open subsets of finite type of $\calM^\ani$ covering the fiber $\calM_a$. By the product formula \ref{produit}, the fiber $\calM_a$ is noetherian, so that there exists a finite family of open subsets of finite type of $\calM^\ani$ covering $\calM_a$. Since $f:\calM^\ani\rightarrow\calA^\ani$ is flat, the images of these open subsets are open subsets of $\calA^\ani$ containing $a$. Writing $V_a$ for their intersection, the open subset $f^{-1}(V_a)$ of $\calM^\ani$ can be covered by a finite family of open subsets of finite type. It remains to note that $\calA^\ani$ is also noetherian, so that there is a finite number of points $a$ such that the open subsets $V_a$ as above cover $\calA^\ani$. The proposition follows.

The assertion on the coarse moduli space now follows from \cite[II.5]{Fa} and \cite{Ray}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The $\kappa$-decomposition on $\tilde\calA^\ani$}
\label{subsection : kappa-decomposition}

Recall the notations $\tilde\calM^\ani=\calM\times_\calA\tilde\calA^\ani$, $\tilde\calP^\ani=\calP\times_\calA\tilde\calA^\ani$ and $\tilde f^\ani:\tilde\calM^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$. By \ref{propre}, this morphism is proper and its source is a smooth Deligne-Mumford stack. By Deligne's purity theorem \cite{Weil2}, $\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql$ is a pure complex. By \cite[5.4.5]{BBD}, over $\tilde\calA^\ani$ it is isomorphic to the direct sum of its perverse cohomology sheaves
$$\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql\simeq\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)[-n],$$
where ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)$ is a pure perverse sheaf of weight $n$.

\begin{numero}\label{decomposition tilde f^ani}
Since $\tilde\calP^\ani$ acts on $\tilde\calM^\ani$ over $\tilde\calA^\ani$, it acts on the direct image $\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql$. By the homotopy lemma \cf \cite[3.2.3]{LN}, the action of $\tilde\calP^\ani$ on the perverse cohomology sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)$ factors through the sheaf of finite abelian groups $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$. By \ref{surjectif}, we have a surjective homomorphism
$$\bbX_*\times\tilde\calA^\ani\rightarrow\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$$
which identifies $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$ with an explicit finite quotient of the constant sheaf $\bbX_*$. In particular, for every $\kappa\in\hat\bbT$ one may define a direct factor ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ on which $\bbX_*$ acts through the character $\kappa:\bbX_*\rightarrow\Ql^\times$, so that there is a direct sum decomposition
$${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in\hat\bbT}{}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$$
having only finitely many non-zero factors.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{tilde A_kappa}
Recall that we have a stratification $\tilde\calA^\ani=\bigsqcup_{\psi\in\Psi^\ani}\tilde\calA_\psi$ \cf \ref{ouvert ani tilde A}. For every $\kappa\in\hat\bbT$, the union of the strata $\tilde\calA_\psi$ with $\psi\in\Psi$ such that $\kappa\in\hat\bbT(I_\psi,W_\psi)$ is a closed subset of $\tilde\calA$, by \ref{T(I,W) inclusion} and \ref{I W croissant}. We write $\tilde\calA_\kappa^\ani=\tilde\calA_\kappa\cap\tilde\calA^\ani$ for the trace of this closed subset on $\tilde\calA^\ani$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}\label{contenu dans tilde A kappa}
The support of the perverse sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ is contained in $\tilde\calA_\kappa^\ani$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
It follows from the explicit description \ref{pi} of $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$ that the restriction of ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ to the open subset $\tilde\calA^\ani-\tilde\calA_\kappa^\ani$ is zero.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The Closed Immersion of $\tilde\calA_H$ into $\tilde\calA$}
\label{subsection : immersion fermee}

Let $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$ be a pointed endoscopic datum of $G$ over $\bar X$, that is, a continuous homomorphism $\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa)$ as in \ref{donnee endoscopique pointee}. We then have a $\pi_0(\kappa)$-torsor $\rho_\kappa:\bar X_{\rho_\kappa}\rightarrow\bar X$ with a point $\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$ above $\infty$. Recall that one may identify $\frakc_D$ with the quotient in the sense of invariants of $\bar X_{\rho_\kappa}\times_{\bar X}\frakt_D$ by the action of $\bbW\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$. We have also constructed a homomorphism $\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)\rightarrow\bbW\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$ \cf \ref{homomorphisme produit semi-direct} such that the quotient in the sense of invariants of $\bar X_{\rho_\kappa}\times_{\bar X}\frakt_D$ by $\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$ is $\frakc_{H,D}$. We have thus deduced a morphism $\frakc_{H,D}\rightarrow\frakc_D$ and hence a morphism $\nu:\calA_H\rightarrow\calA$ \cf \ref{subsection : Fibration de Hitchin de groupes endoscopiques}.

\begin{numero}\label{immense diagramme}
For every $a\in\calA^\infty(\bar k)$ one forms, as in \ref{tilde X rho a}, the cartesian product
$$\widetilde X_{\rho_\kappa,a}=\widetilde X_a\times_{\bar X}\bar X_{\rho_\kappa}.$$
This is a curve traced on $\bar X_{\rho_\kappa}\times_{\bar X}\frakt_D$ whose projection to $\bar X$ is generically \'etale Galois with Galois group $\bbW\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$. The choice of a point $\tilde\infty\in\widetilde X_a$ above $\infty$ is equivalent to the choice of a point $\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$ above $\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$, so that it is legitimate to write $\tilde a=(a,\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa})\in\tilde\calA$ with $a\in\calA^\infty$ and $\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$ as above.

By construction of the morphism $\nu:\calA_H\rightarrow\calA$, if $a_H\in\calA_H$ and $\nu(a_H)=a$, one has an embedding $\widetilde X_{\rho_\kappa,a_H}\rightarrow\widetilde X_{\rho_\kappa,a}$ which realizes the former as a union of certain irreducible components of the latter. One defines a morphism
$$\tilde\nu:\tilde\calA_H\rightarrow\tilde\calA$$
by the recipe $(a_H,\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa})\mapsto(\nu(a_H),\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa})$. Note that if the point $\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$ is defined over $k$, this morphism is also defined over $k$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}\label{immersion fermee}
The morphism $\tilde\nu:\tilde\calA_H\rightarrow\tilde\calA$ is a closed immersion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We know by \cite[10.3]{N} that it is a finite unramified morphism. It now suffices to check that it induces an injection at the level of geometric points.

Suppose that a point $\tilde a=(a,\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa})\in\tilde\calA(\bar k)$ lies in the image of $\tilde\nu$. We shall show that it comes from a unique point $\tilde a_H=(a_H,\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa})\in\tilde\calA_H$. For this, note that $a_H$ is completely determined by the curve $\widetilde X_{\rho_\kappa,a_H}$, and that this curve is the smallest union of irreducible components of $\widetilde X_{\rho_\kappa,a}$ which is stable under $\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$ and which contains the point $\tilde\infty_{\rho_\kappa}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{Galois}
The closed subscheme $\tilde\calA_\kappa$ of $\tilde\calA$ defined in \ref{tilde A_kappa} is the disjoint union of the closed subsets $\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)$ associated with the homomorphisms $\rho_\kappa^\bullet:\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In \ref{WIPHI} we attached to a geometric point $\tilde a=(a,\tilde\infty)\in\tilde\calA(\bar k)$ a diagram
$$
\xymatrix{
\pi_1(U,\infty) \ar[d] \ar[r]^-{\pi_{\tilde a}^\bullet} & \bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG) \ar[d] \\
\pi_1(\bar X,x) \ar[r]_-{\rho_G^\bullet} & \Out(\GG)
}
$$
Here $U$ is the largest open subset of $\bar X$ containing $\infty$ above which the cameral cover $\widetilde X_a\rightarrow\bar X$ is \'etale. We wrote $W_{\tilde a}$ for the image of $\pi_{\tilde a}^\bullet$ in $\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG)$ and $I_{\tilde a}$ for the image of the kernel of the homomorphism $\pi_1(U,\infty)\rightarrow\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)$.

By definition, $(a,\tilde\infty)$ belongs to $\tilde\calA_\kappa$ if and only if $W_{\tilde a}$ is contained in $(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa$ and $I_{\tilde a}$ is contained in the Weyl group $\bbW_\HH$ of the neutral component $\hat\HH$ of the centralizer of $\kappa$ in $\hat\GG$. Recall that one has an exact sequence
\begin{equation}\label{triangle commutatif}
1\rightarrow\bbW_\HH\rightarrow(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa)\rightarrow 1.
\end{equation}
By \cite[Lemma~10.1]{N} there exists a canonical splitting which allows one to identify $(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa$ with a semi-direct product $\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$.

Let $H$ be the endoscopic group associated with a homomorphism $\rho_\kappa^\bullet:\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa)$. Let $\tilde a_H\in\tilde\calA_H(\bar k)$, and let $U$ be the open subset of $\bar X$ containing $\infty$ above which the cameral cover $\widetilde X_{a_H}\rightarrow\bar X$ is \'etale. The homomorphism
$$\pi_{\tilde a_H}^\bullet:\pi_1(U,\infty)\rightarrow\bbW_\HH\rtimes\Out(\HH)$$
induces a homomorphism
$$\pi_{\tilde a_H}^{\kappa,\bullet}:\pi_1(U,\infty)\rightarrow\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$$
over $\rho_\kappa^\bullet:\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa)$. By construction of $\tilde a=\tilde\nu(\tilde a_H)$, the homomorphism $\pi_{\tilde a}^\bullet$ is obtained by composing $\pi_{\tilde a_H}^{\kappa,\bullet}$ with the embedding $\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)\rightarrow\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG)$ via the identification $(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa=\bbW_\HH\rtimes\pi_0(\kappa)$. We therefore have $W_{\tilde a}\subset(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa$ and $I_{\tilde a}\subset\bbW_\HH$, that is, $\tilde a\in\tilde\calA_\kappa(\bar k)$.

Conversely, let $\tilde a\in\tilde\calA_\kappa(\bar k)$. Since $W_{\tilde a}\subset(\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG))_\kappa$ and $I_{\tilde a}\subset\bbW_\HH$, the homomorphism $\pi_{\tilde a}^\bullet$ induces a homomorphism
$$\rho_\kappa^\bullet:\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rightarrow W_{\tilde a}/I_{\tilde a}\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa).$$
Let $H$ be the endoscopic group associated with this $\rho_\kappa^\bullet$. It remains to construct a point $\tilde a_H\in\tilde\calA_H(\bar k)$ such that $\tilde\nu(\tilde a_H)=\tilde a$. This construction is identical to the one appearing in the proof of the preceding proposition.
\end{proof}

Similar arguments allow a natural interpretation of the complement of the open subset $\tilde\calA^\ani$.

\begin{proposition}\label{complement de A ani}
The complement of the open subset $\tilde\calA^\ani$ in $\tilde\calA$ is the union of the images of the closed immersions
$$\tilde\calA_M\rightarrow\tilde\calA$$
over the set of Levi subgroups $M$ containing the maximal torus $T$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $\tilde a\in(\tilde\calA-\tilde\calA^\ani)(\bar k)$. By definition the group $\bbT^{W_{\tilde a}}$ is not finite, and therefore contains a torus $S$. The centralizer of this torus in $G$ is a Levi subgroup $M$ of $G$. The group $W_{\tilde a}$ is a subgroup of the fixer of $S$ in $\bbW\rtimes\Out(\GG)$. The group $I_{\tilde a}$ is contained in the intersection of the fixer of $M$ with $\bbW$, hence contained in $\bbW_M$. The same argument as the one used in the proof of \ref{Galois} shows that $\tilde a$ comes from a point $\tilde a_M\in\tilde\calA_M(\bar k)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{codim complement de A ani}
The codimension of $\calA^\heartsuit-\calA^\ani$ in $\calA^\heartsuit$ is greater than or equal to $\deg(D)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By the description of the complement of $\tilde\calA^\ani$ and the dimension formula \ref{dim A}, we have
$$\dim(\calA)-\dim(\calA_M)=(\sharp\,\Phi-\sharp\,\Phi_M)\deg(D)/2\geq \deg(D),$$
whence the proposition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Geometric Stabilization}
\label{subsection : stabilisation sur tilde A}

By \ref{contenu dans tilde A kappa}, the support of the perverse sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ is contained in $\tilde\calA_\kappa^\ani$. By the description of $\tilde\calA_\kappa^\ani$ in terms of endoscopic groups \cf \ref{Galois}, this perverse sheaf decomposes as a direct sum of factors parametrized by the set of pointed endoscopic data $(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})$, the direct factor parametrized by $\xi$ being supported on the closed subset $\tilde\nu_\xi(\tilde\calA_{H_\xi})\cap\tilde\calA^\ani$, where $H_\xi$ is the endoscopic group attached to $(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})$ and $\tilde\nu_\xi$ is the closed immersion of $\tilde\calA_{H_\xi}$ into $\tilde\calA$. Our main result is a description of each of these direct factors.

\begin{theoreme}\label{stabilisation sur tilde A geom}
There exists an isomorphism between the graded perverse sheaves
$$\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa[2r_H^G(D)](r_H^G(D))$$
and
$$\bigoplus_n \bigoplus_{(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})} \tilde\nu_{\xi,*}\,{}^p\rmH^*(\tilde f^\ani_{H_\xi,*}\Ql)_{\rm st},$$
where the last direct sum runs over the set of all pointed endoscopic data $(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})$ having the fixed component $\kappa$. Here the index ${\rm st}$ denotes the direct factor on which $\tilde\calP_{H_\xi}$ acts trivially, and the integer $r_H^G(D)$ is the codimension of $\tilde\calA_{H_\xi}$ in $\tilde\calA$, given by the formula
$$r_H^G(D)=(\sharp\,\Phi-\sharp\,\Phi_\HH)\deg(D)/2.$$
\end{theoreme}

Although the statement above is over $\bar k$, our proof is in part arithmetic. Suppose now that we have a pointed endoscopic datum $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$ defined over $X$, that is, $\rho_\kappa^\bullet$ extends to a homomorphism
$$\pi_1(X,\infty)=\pi_1(\bar X,\infty)\rtimes\Gal(\bar k/k)\rightarrow\pi_0(\kappa).$$
The associated endoscopic group $H$ is then defined over $X$. We have a Hitchin fibration $f^H:\calM_H\rightarrow\calA_H$ for the group $H$ and an \'etale cover $\tilde\calA_H$ of $\calA_H$. The closed immersion $\tilde\nu:\tilde\calA_H\rightarrow\tilde\calA_G$ of \ref{immersion fermee} is then defined over $k$.

\begin{theoreme}\label{stabilisation sur tilde A}
There exists an isomorphism defined over $k$ between the semi-simplifications of the graded perverse sheaves
$$\bigoplus_n \tilde\nu^*\,{}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa[2r_H^G(D)](r_H^G(D))\simeq\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^*(\tilde f^\ani_{H,*}\Ql)_{\rm st}.$$
\end{theoreme}

Note that, both sides of the equality being graded pure perverse sheaves, they are geometrically semisimple \cf \cite{BBD}. It follows that the graded perverse sheaves above are isomorphic over $\tilde\calA^\ani\otimes_k\bar k$. Moreover, since every endoscopic datum over $\bar X$ is defined over $X\otimes_k k'$ for some finite extension $k'$ of $k$, Theorem \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A geom} is a consequence of \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}. The proof of the latter is not completed until paragraph \ref{subsection : stabilisation geometrique}. We shall obtain the Langlands-Shelstad conjecture \ref{LS} in particular in the course of its proof.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
% [journal 6.5] "Cohomologie ordinaire de degre maximal". ENTIRELY MISSING FROM THE
% arXiv VERSION, whose chapter 6 ended with the complement subsection now folded into 6.3.
% 6.5.1 is what makes the support theorem effective: it is cited twice in 7.8.
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Ordinary Cohomology of Maximal Degree}\label{subsection : cohomologie ordinaire max}

In this paragraph we prove an elementary variant of the geometric stabilization theorem \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A geom}, in which, instead of the perverse cohomology sheaves, one considers the ordinary cohomology sheaf of maximal degree.

Write $d$ for the relative dimension of the morphism $\tilde f^\ani:\tilde\calM^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$. By the homotopy lemma, the action of $\tilde\calP^\ani$ on $\rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql$ factors through the finite sheaf $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$. Recall also that the latter is a quotient of the constant sheaf $\bbX_*$. We write $(\rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_{\rm st}$ for the largest direct factor on which $\bbX_*$ acts trivially, and $(\rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ for the one on which it acts through the character $\kappa:\bbX_*\rightarrow\Ql^\times$ that we have fixed.

\begin{proposition}\label{cohomologie ordinaire max}
There is an isomorphism between the stable part $(\rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_{\rm st}$ and the constant sheaf $\Ql$. There is also an isomorphism between $(\rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ and the direct sum
$$\bigoplus_{(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})}\tilde\nu_{\xi,*}\Ql$$
extended over the set of pointed endoscopic data $(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})$ having the fixed component $\kappa$, where $\tilde\nu_\xi$ denotes the closed immersion of $\tilde\calA_{H_\xi}^\ani$ into $\tilde\calA^\ani$, with $H_\xi$ the endoscopic group attached to $(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Hitchin-Kostant section defines an open immersion $\tilde\calP^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calM^\ani$ whose closed complement has relative dimension $\leq d-1$ \cf \ref{densite globale}. There follows an isomorphism
$$\rmR^{2d}g_!\Ql\rightarrow \rmR^{2d}\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql$$
compatible with the action of $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$. Here $g$ denotes the morphism $\tilde\calP^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$.

The trace morphism allows one to identify $\rmR^{2d}g_!\Ql$ with the sheaf associated with the presheaf
$$U\mapsto \Ql^{\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)(U)}.$$
The proposition therefore follows from the explicit description of the sheaf $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$ given in \ref{pi}. Indeed, $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$ is a quotient of the constant sheaf $\bbX_*$. One may replace $\bbX_*$ by a finite quotient $\bbX$. Thus $\rmR^{2d}g_!\Ql$ is a quotient of the constant sheaf $\Ql^\bbX$. The isotypic parts $(\rmR^{2d}g_!\Ql)_{\rm st}$ and $(\rmR^{2d}g_!\Ql)_\kappa$ are quotients of the corresponding isotypic parts of $\Ql^\bbX$, which are constant sheaves of rank one. The assertions to be proved are now reduced to a fiber-by-fiber verification, which is easy.
\end{proof}

%===================================================================================
% CHAPTER 7 -- rewritten to match the journal version (Publ. IHES 111 (2010), pp. 111-135).
% The arXiv chapter 7 was organised around "amplitude"; the journal reorganises it into
% 7.1 Abelian fibration / 7.2 Statement / 7.3 Goresky-MacPherson inequality /
% 7.4 Cap-product and freeness / 7.5 Freeness in the punctual case /
% 7.6 Study over a henselian base / 7.7 Freeness by induction / 7.8 The Hitchin fibration.
%===================================================================================
\section{The Support Theorem} \label{support}

Let $S$ be a $k$-scheme of finite type and $f:M\rightarrow S$ a proper morphism whose source is smooth. By the purity \cite{Weil2} and decomposition \cite{BBD} theorems, the complex $f_*\Ql$ is pure and is isomorphic, over $S\otimes_k\bar k$, to a direct sum of geometrically simple perverse sheaves with shifts. Their supports constitute an important topological invariant of $f$. In general, it is difficult to determine this invariant explicitly.

We introduce a notion of {\em $\delta$-regular abelian fibration} \cf \ref{delta regulier} for which it is possible to determine the supports \cf \ref{support general}. Since we do not know how to prove the $\delta$-regularity of the Hitchin fibration in positive characteristic, we formulate the statements \ref{inegalite delta} and \ref{support general kappa}, which give sufficient control
for the proof of the theorems \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A geom} and \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}.. With the exception of the last paragraph, this chapter is written in the general situation of weak abelian fibrations.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Abelian Fibration}\label{subsection : fibration abelienne}

We are going to formalize the properties observed on the Hitchin fibration in order to axiomatize a notion of algebraic abelian fibration. We shall in fact introduce, on the one hand, the notion of {\em weak abelian fibration}, which gathers together those properties that are stable under arbitrary base change, and on the other hand that of {\em $\delta$-regular abelian fibration}, which is preserved only under flat base change. The right notion of abelian fibration ought to lie somewhere between the two.

\begin{numero}\label{fibration abelienne faible}
A {\em weak abelian fibration} over a $k$-scheme $S$ consists of a proper morphism $f:M\rightarrow S$ together with a smooth commutative group scheme $g:P\rightarrow S$ equipped with an action
$$\act:P\times_S M\rightarrow M$$
satisfying the three properties \ref{meme dimension}, \ref{stabilisateur affine} and \ref{module de Tate polarisable} below.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{meme dimension}
The morphisms $f$ and $g$ have the same relative dimension $d$.\footnote{This hypothesis is in no way necessary, but it simplifies the numerology.}
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{stabilisateur affine}
The action of $P$ on $M$ has only affine stabilizers; that is, for every geometric point $m\in M$ lying over $s\in S$, the stabilizer of $m$ in $P_s$ is an affine subgroup.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{module de Tate polarisable}
% [journal 7.1.4] The journal states this for the neutral-component subgroup scheme P^0 and
% the morphism g^0, NOT for P and g as the arXiv version did. Restored here.
Let $P^0$ be the open subgroup scheme of neutral components of the fibers of $P$, and write $g^0:P^0\rightarrow S$. Consider the sheaf of Tate modules
$$\rmT_\Ql(P^0)=\rmH^{2d-1}(g^0_! \Ql)(d)$$
whose fiber over each geometric point $s$ of $S$ is the $\Ql$-Tate module $\rmT_\Ql(P^0_s)$. For every geometric point $s$ of $S$, the canonical Chevalley d\'evissage of $P^0_s$
$$1\rightarrow R_s \rightarrow P^0_s \rightarrow A_s \rightarrow 1,$$
in which $A_s$ is an abelian variety and $R_s$ is a connected commutative affine algebraic group, induces a d\'evissage of Tate modules \cf \cite{SGA7}
$$0\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(R_s)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P^0_s) \rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_s)\rightarrow 0.$$

We shall say that $\rmT_\Ql(P^0)$ is {\em polarizable} if, locally for the \'etale topology on $S$, there exists an alternating bilinear form
% [journal 7.1.4] target is \Ql, not \Ql(1) as in the arXiv version: the Tate twist (d)
% is already built into the definition of \rmT_\Ql(P^0) above.
$$\rmT_\Ql(P^0)\times \rmT_\Ql(P^0)\rightarrow \Ql$$
whose fiber at each geometric point $s$ has $\rmT_\Ql(R_s)$ as its kernel, that is, which annihilates $\rmT_\Ql(R_s)$ and induces a perfect pairing on $\rmT_\Ql(A_s)$.
\end{numero}

The three properties \ref{meme dimension}, \ref{stabilisateur affine} and \ref{module de Tate polarisable} are preserved under arbitrary base change. In particular, the generic fiber of a weak abelian fibration need not be an abelian variety. We now introduce a strong restriction, called $\delta$-regularity, which will guarantee this property among others.

\begin{numero}\label{delta regulier}
For every geometric point $s\in S$, write $\delta_s=\dim(R_s)$ for the dimension of the affine part of $P_s$. If $s\in S$ is an arbitrary point, the Chevalley d\'evissage of $P_s$ exists and is unique after a radicial base change, so that the integer $\delta_s$ is well defined. The function $\delta$, defined on the topological space underlying $S$ with values in the natural numbers, is semi-continuous \cf \ref{delta semicontinu}. Assuming this function to be constructible, there exists a stratification of $S$ into locally closed subschemes $S_\delta$ such that $\delta_s=\delta$ for every geometric point $s\in S_\delta$.

We shall say that the smooth commutative $S$-group scheme $P$ is {\em $\delta$-regular} if for every $\delta\in\NN$ we have
$${\rm codim}_S(S_\delta)\geq \delta.$$
If $S_\delta$ is empty, our convention assigns the value infinity to the codimension.

A weak abelian fibration whose component $P$ enjoys $\delta$-regularity will be called a {\em $\delta$-regular abelian fibration}.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{delta regulier bis}
There is another formulation of the notion of $\delta$-regularity. Let $Z$ be an irreducible closed subscheme of $S$ and let $\delta_Z$ be the minimal value taken by the function $\delta$ on $Z$. Then $P$ is $\delta$-regular if and only if, for every irreducible closed subscheme $Z$ of $S$, we have ${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_Z$.

Indeed, since the value $\delta_Z$ is attained on a dense open subset of $Z$, the subscheme $Z$ is contained in the closure of the stratum $S_{\delta_Z}$. If $P$ is $\delta$-regular, then ${\rm codim}(S_{\delta_Z})\geq \delta_Z$ and a fortiori ${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_Z$. The other implication is equally clear.

Note that $\delta$-regularity is preserved under flat base change. Note also that $\delta$-regularity implies
$${\rm codim}(S_1)\geq 1,$$
so that $S_0\not=\emptyset$. It follows that $P^0$ is generically an abelian variety.
\end{numero}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Statement of the Support Theorem}\label{subsection : support general}

Let $S$ be a $k$-scheme of finite type and $f:M\rightarrow S$ a proper morphism. Suppose that the constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $M$ is self-dual, and hence pure; this is in particular the case if $M$ is a smooth $k$-scheme. By Deligne's purity theorem, the direct image complex $f_*\Ql$ is pure. Applying the decomposition theorem \cite{BBD}, we know that over $S\otimes_k\bar k$ the complex $f_*\Ql$ is isomorphic to the direct sum of its perverse cohomology sheaves with the evident shifts
$$f_*\Ql \simeq \bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)[-n],$$
and moreover the perverse sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ are semisimple. By \cite{BBD}, for every geometrically simple perverse sheaf $K$ on $S\otimes_k\bar k$ there exist an irreducible reduced closed subscheme $i:Z\hookrightarrow S\otimes_k\bar k$, a dense open subset $j:U\hookrightarrow Z$ and an irreducible local system $\calK$ on $U$ such that
$$K=i_* j_{!*} \calK[\dim(Z)].$$
The closed subscheme $Z$ is completely determined by $K$ and will be called the {\em support} of $K$. In general, the problem of determining the supports of the simple perverse sheaves occurring in the decomposition of $f_*\Ql$ is a very difficult one. It can however be solved in the case of a $\delta$-regular abelian fibration.

\begin{theoreme}\label{support general}
Let $S$ be a geometrically irreducible $k$-scheme of finite type. Let $f:M\rightarrow S$ be a projective morphism, purely of relative dimension $d$, equipped with an action of a smooth group scheme $g:P\rightarrow S$ forming a $\delta$-regular abelian fibration. Suppose that the constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $M$ is self-dual, and hence pure.

Let $K$ be a geometrically simple perverse sheaf occurring in the decomposition of one of the perverse cohomology sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$, and let $Z$ be its support. Then there exist an open subset $U$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ with $U\cap Z$ non-empty, and a non-trivial local system $L$ on $U\cap Z$, such that $i_*L$, where $i$ denotes the closed immersion $U\cap Z\rightarrow U$, is a direct factor of the restriction to $U$ of the ordinary cohomology sheaf of maximal degree $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)$.
\end{theoreme}

Despite its complicated appearance, this statement is in fact effective for determining the supports, because the ordinary cohomology sheaf of maximal degree is in general known \cf \ref{subsection : cohomologie ordinaire max}, as are its local direct factors. Note also that $K$ and $L$ have nothing in common except their support. The proof of the theorem passes through the following statement.

\begin{proposition}\label{inegalite delta}
Let $S$ be a geometrically irreducible $k$-scheme of finite type, and let $f:M\rightarrow S$ be a projective morphism, purely of relative dimension $d$, equipped with an action of a smooth group scheme $g:P\rightarrow S$ forming a weak abelian fibration. Suppose that the constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $M$ is self-dual, and hence pure.

Let $K$ be a geometrically simple perverse sheaf occurring in the decomposition of one of the perverse cohomology sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$, and let $Z$ be its support. Let $\delta_Z$ be the minimal value taken on $Z$ by the function $\delta$ attached to $P$. Then one has the inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq \delta_Z.$$
If equality is attained, then there exist an open subset $U$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ with $U\cap Z$ non-empty and a non-trivial local system $L$ on $U\cap Z$ such that $i_*L$, where $i$ denotes the closed immersion $U\cap Z\rightarrow U$, is a direct factor of the restriction to $U$ of the ordinary cohomology sheaf of maximal degree $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)$.
\end{proposition}

It is clear that \ref{inegalite delta} implies \ref{support general}, since the hypothesis of $\delta$-regularity yields the opposite inequality ${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_Z$ \cf \ref{delta regulier bis}.

Consider a variant of the delta inequality \ref{inegalite delta} which takes the groups of connected components of the fibers of $P$ into account as well. Let $\pi_0(P)$ be the sheaf of groups of connected components of the fibers of $P$. Suppose that it is a quotient of a constant sheaf with fiber the finite abelian group $\bbX$, as for instance in \ref{pi}.

The group scheme $P$ acts on the perverse cohomology sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ through $\pi_0(P)$. The group $\bbX$ therefore acts on ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$. For every character $\kappa:\bbX\rightarrow\Ql^\times$ we have the largest direct factor ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$ of ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ on which $\bbX$ acts through $\kappa$. Likewise, we have the direct factor $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$ of the ordinary cohomology sheaf $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)$.

In fact, there exist a natural number $N$ and a decomposition of the bounded constructible complex $f_*\Ql$
$$f_*\Ql=\bigoplus_{\kappa\in\bbX^*} (f_*\Ql)_\kappa$$
such that for every $\alpha\in\bbX$ the endomorphism $(\alpha-\kappa(\alpha)\id)^N$ vanishes on the direct factor $(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$ \cf \cite[3.2.5]{N}.
If one replaces $f_*\Ql$ by $(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$ in the proof of \ref{inegalite delta}, one obtains the following variant.

\begin{proposition}\label{support general kappa}
In \ref{inegalite delta} one may replace the perverse sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ by its isotypic factor ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$, and the ordinary sheaf $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)$ by its isotypic factor $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)_\kappa$.
\end{proposition}

The reader will note that, just as in \ref{inegalite delta}, we do not assume here that $P$ is $\delta$-regular.

In the last paragraph of this chapter we shall apply \ref{support general kappa} to the case of the Hitchin fibration, more precisely to the morphism $\tilde f^\ani:\tilde\calM^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$. The rest of the chapter is devoted to proving the delta inequality \ref{inegalite delta}.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The Goresky-MacPherson Inequality}\label{subsection : inegalite GM}

Goresky and MacPherson observed that Poincar\'e duality imposes a constraint on the codimension of the support of the simple perverse sheaves occurring in the decomposition theorem. This is a crucial observation, of which our delta inequality \ref{inegalite delta} is a variant. We begin by recalling their original inequality.

\begin{theoreme}\label{inegalite GM}
Let $S$ be a geometrically irreducible $k$-scheme of finite type. Let $f:M\rightarrow S$ be a proper morphism, purely of relative dimension $d$. Suppose that the constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $M$ is self-dual, and hence pure.

Let $K$ be an irreducible perverse sheaf on $S\otimes_k\bar k$ occurring in the decomposition of one of the perverse cohomology sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$, and let $Z$ be the support of $K$. Then one has the inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq d.$$
Suppose that equality holds. Then there exist an open subset $U$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ with $U\cap Z$ non-empty and a non-trivial local system $L$ on $U\cap Z$ such that $i_*L$, where $i$ denotes the closed immersion $U\cap Z\rightarrow U$, is a direct factor of the restriction to $U$ of the ordinary cohomology sheaf of maximal degree $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)$.
\end{theoreme}

\begin{proof}
Let $Z$ be an irreducible closed subscheme of $S\otimes_k\bar k$. Define ${\rm occ}(Z)$ to be the set of integers $n$ such that there exists an irreducible direct factor of the perverse cohomology sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ with support $Z$. By Poincar\'e duality, ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ is the dual of ${}^p\rmH^{2\dim(M)-n}(f_*\Ql)$, so that the set ${\rm occ}(Z)$ is symmetric about the integer $\dim(M)$.

If ${\rm occ}(Z)\not=\emptyset$, there exists an integer $n\geq \dim(M)$ belonging to ${\rm occ}(Z)$. There exist an open subset $U$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ and an irreducible local system $L$ on $U\cap Z$ such that $i_*L[\dim(Z)]$ is a direct factor of ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)|_U$. This implies that $i_*L[\dim(Z)-n]$ is a direct factor of the complex $f_*\Ql|_U$, since the latter is pure. Now passing to the ordinary cohomology sheaf, $i_*L$ becomes a direct factor of $\rmH^{n-\dim(Z)}(f_*\Ql)$. Since the fibers of the morphism $f:M\rightarrow S$ are purely of dimension $d$, we have $\dim(M)=d+\dim(S)$. On the other hand, the non-vanishing of $\rmH^{n-\dim(Z)}(f_*\Ql)$ implies that
$$\dim(M)-\dim(Z)\leq n-\dim(Z)\leq 2d$$
by the theorem on cohomological amplitude. Combining the two pieces of information, we obtain the desired inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq d,$$
together with the necessary condition for equality to hold.
\end{proof}

In the more specific situation of a weak abelian fibration, it is in fact possible to improve the Goresky-MacPherson inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq d$$
to the delta inequality \ref{inegalite delta}
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq \delta_Z.$$
Although the proof of the latter is appreciably more difficult, the underlying topological idea is very simple.

The delta inequality can be reduced to the Goresky-MacPherson inequality if one grants the existence of certain local liftings. Let $s$ be a geometric point of $Z$ such that $\delta_s=\delta_Z$. Denote by $A_s$ the maximal abelian quotient of $P^0_s$. Suppose that there exists an \'etale neighbourhood $S'$ of $s$ in $S$ such that, over $S'$, the abelian variety $A_s$ extends to an abelian scheme $A_{S'}$, and that moreover there exists a homomorphism $A_{S'}\rightarrow P^0_{S'}$ such that at the point $s$ the composite $A_s\rightarrow P^0_s\rightarrow A_s$ is an isogeny of $A_s$. Over $S'$, the abelian scheme $A_{S'}$ acts on $M_{S'}$ with finite stabilizers, in view of hypothesis \ref{stabilisateur affine}. Forming the quotient $[M_{S'}/A_{S'}]$, one factors the morphism $M_{S'}\rightarrow S'$ into a proper and smooth morphism $M_{S'}\rightarrow [M_{S'}/A_{S'}]$ followed by a morphism $[M_{S'}/A_{S'}]\rightarrow S'$ which is purely of relative dimension $\delta_s$. One may then apply the Goresky-MacPherson inequality to the second morphism.

In general these liftings do not exist. The proof of \ref{inegalite delta} consists precisely in transposing the geometric argument above to the cohomological level.
Let $Z$ be an irreducible closed subscheme of $S$. In the proof of \ref{inegalite GM} we introduced the set ${\rm occ}(Z)$ of occurrences of $Z$ as the support of the irreducible factors of the perverse cohomology sheaves ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$. When this set is non-empty, we define
$${\rm amp}(Z)={\rm max}({\rm occ}(Z))-{\rm min}({\rm occ}(Z)).$$
\begin{proposition}\label{inegalite amplitude}
Place ourselves under the hypotheses of \ref{inegalite delta}; in particular ${\rm occ}(Z)\not=\emptyset$. Then one has the inequality
$${\rm amp}(Z)\geq 2(d-\delta_Z).$$
\end{proposition}

We now prove \ref{inegalite delta}, granting \ref{inegalite amplitude}. Poincar\'e duality implies that the set ${\rm occ}(Z)$ is symmetric about $\dim(M)$. The additional constraint ${\rm amp}(Z)\geq 2(d-\delta_Z)$ forces the existence of an integer $n\geq \dim(M)+d-\delta_Z$ belonging to ${\rm occ}(Z)$. The remainder of the proof of \ref{inegalite delta} is now exactly the same as the end of the proof of \ref{inegalite GM}.

The rest of the chapter is devoted to the proof of the amplitude inequality \ref{inegalite amplitude}.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Cap-Product and Freeness}\label{subsection : cap-produit}

We shall develop the construction of the cap-product and state a freeness property which implies the amplitude inequality \ref{inegalite amplitude}.

\begin{numero}\label{complexe homologie P}
We place ourselves in the following general situation. Let $S$ be an arbitrary scheme, and let $g:P\rightarrow S$ be a smooth commutative $S$-group scheme of finite type with connected fibers of dimension $d$. Consider the homology complex of $P$ over $S$, defined by the formula
$$\Lambda_P=g_!\Ql[2d](d).$$
This complex is concentrated in non-positive degrees. In degree $0$ we have $\rmH^0(\Lambda_P)=\Ql$. The most important part of $\Lambda_P$ is
$$\rmT_\Ql(P):=\rmH^{-1}(\Lambda_P),$$
where $\rmT_\Ql(P)$ is a sheaf whose fiber at each geometric point $s\in S$ is the $\Ql$-Tate module $\rmT_\Ql(P_s)$ of the fiber of $P$ at $s$. More generally, the base-change theorem provides an isomorphism
$$\rmH^{-i}(\Lambda_P)_s=\rmH_c^{2d-i}(P_s)(d)$$
between the fiber at $s$ of $\rmH^{-i}(\Lambda_P)$ and the $(2d-i)$-th compactly supported cohomology group
$$\rmH_i(P_s)=\rmH_c^{2d-i}(P_s)(d)$$
of the fiber of $P$ at $s$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{cap-produit}
Let $f:M\rightarrow S$ be a morphism of finite type equipped with an action of $P$ relative to the base $S$
$$\act:P\times_S M\rightarrow M.$$
Since $P$ is smooth of relative dimension $d$ over $S$, the morphism $\act$ is also smooth of the same relative dimension. We therefore have a trace morphism
$$\act_!\Ql[2d](d)\rightarrow \Ql$$
over $M$. Pushing this trace morphism forward by $f_!$, we obtain a morphism
$$(g\times_S f)_!\Ql[2d](d)\rightarrow f_!\Ql.$$
Now using the K\"unneth isomorphism, we obtain a cap-product morphism
$$\Lambda_P\otimes f_!\Ql \rightarrow f_!\Ql.$$
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{algebre graduee homologie}
This construction applies in particular to $f=g$. It then defines a morphism of complexes
$$\Lambda_P\otimes \Lambda_P\rightarrow \Lambda_P.$$
From it we deduce a graded algebra structure on the cohomology sheaves of $\Lambda_P$
$$\rmH^{-i}(\Lambda_P)\otimes \rmH^{-j}(\Lambda_P)\rightarrow \rmH^{-i-j}(\Lambda_P),$$
which is commutative in the graded sense. In particular we deduce a morphism of sheaves
$$\wedge^i \rmT_\Ql(P)\rightarrow \rmH^{-i}(\Lambda_P),$$
which is in fact an isomorphism. To check this it suffices to do so fiber by fiber, where one recovers the homology groups of $P_s$ equipped with the Pontryagin product.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{decomposition Lieberman}
Multiplication by an integer $N\not=0$ in $P$ induces multiplication by $N$ on the Tate module $\rmT_\Ql(P)$ of $P$, and hence induces multiplication by $N^i$ on $\rmH^{-i}(\Lambda_P)=\wedge^i \rmT_\Ql(P)$. Lieberman's trick \cite[2A11]{Kl}, which consists in constructing projectors from linear combinations of these endomorphisms of $\Lambda_P$ associated with different values of $N$, allows one to decompose this complex canonically as
$$\Lambda_P=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0} \wedge^i \rmT_\Ql(P)[i]$$
in a way compatible with the multiplication.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{decomposition par supports}
We shall now study the cap-product action of $\Lambda_P$ on $f_!\Ql$ in the situation of \ref{inegalite delta}. Recall that since $f$ is assumed projective, we have $f_!=f_*$. Moreover, the constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $M$ being assumed pure, the direct image $f_*\Ql$ is also pure and decomposes over $S\otimes_k\bar k$ into a direct sum of irreducible perverse sheaves with shifts.

For every irreducible closed subset $Z$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ we introduced the set ${\rm occ}(Z)$ in the proof of \ref{inegalite GM}. Since $f_*\Ql$ is a bounded constructible complex, the set ${\rm occ}(Z)$ is empty except for finitely many irreducible closed subschemes $Z$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$. We index these by a finite set $\FrakB$, and for every $\alpha\in\FrakB$ we write $Z_\alpha$ for the corresponding irreducible closed subset. For every $n$ we then have the canonical decomposition
$${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K^n_\alpha,$$
where $K^n_\alpha$ is the direct sum of the irreducible perverse factors of ${}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)$ having the irreducible closed subset $Z_\alpha$ as support. We write
$$K_\alpha=\bigoplus_{n\in\ZZ} K^n_\alpha[-n].$$
We assume that $K_\alpha$ is non-zero for every $\alpha\in\FrakB$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{cap-produit module de Tate}
In view of the decomposition \ref{decomposition Lieberman}, we have a cap-product by the Tate module \ref{cap-produit}
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes f_!\Ql \rightarrow f_!\Ql[-1].$$
Applying the functor ${}^p\tau^{\leq n}$ to $f_!\Ql$, we have a morphism
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql) \rightarrow f_!\Ql[-1]$$
for every $n$. Applying the functor ${}^p\rmH^n$, we now obtain an arrow
$${}^p\rmH^n\big(\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql)\big) \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_!\Ql).$$
Note now that $\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n-1}(f_!\Ql)\in {}^p\rmD_c^{\leq n-1}(S,\Ql)$, so that the arrow
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql) \rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)[-n]$$
induces an isomorphism at the level of the $n$-th perverse cohomology sheaves
$${}^p\rmH^n\big(\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql)\big) \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^0\big(\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)\big).$$
From this we deduce an arrow
$${}^p\rmH^0\big(\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)\big) \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_!\Ql).$$
Again, since $\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)\in {}^p\rmD_c^{\leq 0}(S,\Ql)$, we have an arrow from this object into its ${}^p\rmH^0$, which therefore induces a canonical arrow
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql) \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_!\Ql).$$
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{fleche diagonale}
Consider the decomposition by supports
$$\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB}\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes K^n_\alpha \rightarrow \bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K^{n-1}_\alpha.$$
For every $\alpha\in\FrakB$ we therefore have a diagonal arrow
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes K^n_\alpha \rightarrow K^{n-1}_\alpha.$$
Note that it is not excluded that the non-diagonal arrows
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes K^n_\alpha \rightarrow K^{n-1}_{\alpha'}$$
be non-zero.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{ouvert V alpha}
For every $\alpha\in\FrakB$ there exists a dense open subset $V_\alpha$ of $Z_\alpha$ such that the restriction of the perverse sheaf $K^n_\alpha$ to $V_\alpha$ is of the form $\calK^n_\alpha[\dim(V_\alpha)]$, where $\calK^n_\alpha$ is a pure local system of weight $n$.

After possibly shrinking the open subset $V_\alpha$, there exists a finite radicial base change $V'_\alpha\rightarrow V_\alpha$ such that the group scheme $P|_{V'_\alpha}$ admits a d\'evissage
$$1\rightarrow R_\alpha \rightarrow P|_{V'_\alpha}\rightarrow A_\alpha \rightarrow 1,$$
where $A_\alpha$ is a $V'_\alpha$-abelian scheme and $R_\alpha$ is a smooth commutative affine $V'_\alpha$-group scheme with connected fibers. From this we deduce an exact sequence of Tate modules
$$0\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(R_\alpha)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P|_{V'_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)\rightarrow 0.$$
Since the morphism $V'_\alpha\rightarrow V_\alpha$ is a homeomorphism, it is legitimate to regard $\rmT_\Ql(R_\alpha)$ and $\rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)$, as well as the exact sequence above, as objects existing over $V_\alpha$. As $A_\alpha$ is an abelian scheme, the Tate module $\rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)$ is a pure local system of weight $-1$. After shrinking $V_\alpha$ further if necessary, we may assume that $\rmT_\Ql(R_\alpha)$, which is the Tate module of the toric part of $R_\alpha$, is a pure local system of weight $-2$. Here the weights in question are relative to a sufficiently large extension of the finite base field $k$ over which our objects are defined.

After shrinking $V_\alpha$ if necessary, we may assume that unless $Z_\alpha$ is entirely contained in $Z_{\alpha'}$, we have $V_\alpha\cap Z_{\alpha'}=\emptyset$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{structure de module gradue}
Let $V_\alpha$ be a dense open subset of $Z_\alpha$ as in \ref{ouvert V alpha}. For every $\alpha\in\FrakB$, choose a Zariski open subset $U_\alpha$ of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ containing $V_\alpha$ as a closed subscheme. Write $i_\alpha:V_\alpha\rightarrow U_\alpha$ for the closed immersion. Restricting the diagonal arrow constructed in \ref{fleche diagonale} to the open subset $U_\alpha$, we obtain an arrow
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes i_{\alpha*}\calK^n_\alpha[\dim(V_\alpha)] \rightarrow i_{\alpha*}\calK^{n-1}_\alpha[\dim(V_\alpha)].$$
By the projection formula we have
% [journal, p. 120] The journal prints the right-hand side with \calK_\alpha; it should read
% \calK^n_\alpha, as the following display confirms. Corrected here.
$$\rmT_\Ql(P)\otimes i_{\alpha*}\calK^n_\alpha = i_{\alpha*}\big(i_\alpha^*\rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)\otimes \calK^n_\alpha\big).$$
Applying the functor $i_\alpha^*$ to
$$i_{\alpha*}\big(i_\alpha^*\rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)\otimes \calK^n_\alpha\big) \rightarrow i_{\alpha*}\calK^{n-1}_\alpha,$$
we obtain a morphism
$$i_\alpha^*\rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)\otimes \calK^n_\alpha \rightarrow \calK^{n-1}_\alpha$$
over $V_\alpha$.

By \ref{ouvert V alpha} we have a d\'evissage of $\rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)$
$$0\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(R_\alpha)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)\rightarrow 0,$$
where $\rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)$ is a pure local system of weight $-1$ and $\rmT_\Ql(R_\alpha)$ is a pure local system of weight $-2$. Since $\calK^n_\alpha$ is pure of weight $n$ and $\calK^{n-1}_\alpha$ is pure of weight $n-1$, the action of $\rmT_\Ql(P_\alpha)$ factors through $\rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)$
$$\rmT_\Ql(A_\alpha)\otimes \calK^n_\alpha \rightarrow \calK^{n-1}_\alpha.$$
We have thus endowed the direct sum of local systems $\calK_\alpha=\bigoplus_n \calK^n_\alpha[-n]$ with the structure of a graded module over the graded algebra of local systems $\Lambda_{A_\alpha}$
$$\Lambda_{A_\alpha}\otimes \calK_\alpha \rightarrow \calK_\alpha.$$
In particular, for every geometric point $u_\alpha$ of $V_\alpha$, the fiber $\calK_{\alpha,u_\alpha}$ is a graded module over the graded algebra $\Lambda_{A_\alpha,u_\alpha}$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}\label{liberte}
Place ourselves under the hypotheses of \ref{inegalite delta} and with the notations of \ref{decomposition par supports}, \ref{ouvert V alpha} and \ref{structure de module gradue}. For every geometric point $u_\alpha$ of $V_\alpha$, the fiber $\calK_{\alpha,u_\alpha}$ of $\calK_\alpha$ is a free graded module over the graded algebra $\Lambda_{A_\alpha,u_\alpha}$.
\end{proposition}

The amplitude inequality \ref{inegalite amplitude} is an immediate consequence of this freeness property. Indeed, ${\rm amp}(Z_\alpha)$ is then at least equal to
$$2\dim(A_\alpha)=2(d-\delta_\alpha).$$

The rest of the chapter is devoted to the proof of the freeness property \ref{liberte}. But before embarking on the proof proper of \ref{liberte}, we observe that the property that $\calK_{\alpha,u_\alpha}$ be a free module over $\Lambda_{A_\alpha,u_\alpha}$ is independent of the choice of the geometric point $u_\alpha\in V_\alpha$. We shall prove the following statement, which allows one to formulate this freeness property independently of the base point. Observe that under the purity hypotheses of \ref{liberte}, the local systems $\calK^n_\alpha$ are geometrically semisimple.

\begin{lemma}\label{liberte sans point base}
Let $U$ be a connected $\bar k$-scheme. Let $\Lambda$ be a graded local system in finitely many non-positive degrees with $\Lambda^0=\Ql$, equipped with a graded algebra structure $\Lambda\otimes \Lambda\rightarrow \Lambda$. Let $L$ be a graded local system equipped with a graded module structure
$$\Lambda\otimes L\rightarrow L.$$
Suppose that there exists a geometric point $u$ of $U$ such that the fiber $L_u$ of $L$ at $u$ is a free module over the fiber $\Lambda_u$ of $\Lambda$ at $u$. Suppose that $L$ is semisimple as a graded local system. Then there exist a graded local system $E$ on $U$ and an isomorphism
$$L=\Lambda\otimes E$$
compatible with the $\Lambda$-module structures.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Consider the augmentation ideal of $\Lambda$
$$\Lambda^+=\bigoplus_{i>0}\Lambda^{-i}[i].$$
Write $E$ for the cokernel of the arrow
$$\Lambda^+\otimes L\rightarrow L.$$
Since $L$ is semisimple, the surjective homomorphism $L\rightarrow E$ admits a lifting $E\rightarrow L$. We shall show that the induced arrow
$$\Lambda\otimes E\rightarrow L$$
is an isomorphism. Since this is an arrow between local systems, in order to check that it is an isomorphism it suffices to check that its fiber at $u$ is one. In the vector space $L_u$, the subspace $E_u$ is a complement of $\Lambda^+L_u$. The arrow
$$\Lambda_u\otimes E_u\rightarrow L_u$$
is therefore surjective by Nakayama's lemma. It is bijective because, $L_u$ being a free $\Lambda_u$-module, one has the equality of dimensions
$$\dim_\Ql(\Lambda_u)\times \dim_\Ql(E_u)=\dim_\Ql(L_u),$$
whence the lemma.
\end{proof}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The Freeness Property in the Punctual Case}\label{subsection : liberte ponctuel}

We begin by considering statements analogous to \ref{liberte} in the case where the base is a point.

\begin{proposition}\label{liberte ponctuel}
Let $M$ be a projective scheme over an algebraically closed field $\bar k$ equipped with an action of a $\bar k$-abelian variety $A$ with finite stabilizers. Then
$$\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M\otimes_k \bar k)[-n]$$
is a free module over the graded algebra $\Lambda_A=\bigoplus_i \wedge^i\rmT_\Ql(A)[i]$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since the stabilizer in $A$ of every point of $M$ is a finite subgroup, the quotient $N=[M/A]$ is a proper algebraic stack over $k$ with finite inertia. Since $M$ is projective, the morphism $m:M\rightarrow N$ is a projective and smooth morphism. The cup-product with the hyperplane class induces, by Deligne \cite{D-dec}, an isomorphism
$$m_*\Ql \simeq \bigoplus_i \rmR^i m_*\Ql[-i],$$
where the $\rmR^i(m_*\Ql)$ are local systems on $N$. Since $M\times_N M=A\times M$, the inverse image $m^*\rmR^i(m_*\Ql)$ is the constant sheaf on $M$ with value $\rmH^i(A)$. Using \cite[4.2.5]{BBD}, we obtain a canonical isomorphism between $\rmR^i(m_*\Ql)$ and the constant sheaf on $N$ with value $\rmH^i(A)$.

The direct sum decomposition above implies the degeneration of the spectral sequence
$$\rmH^j_c(N,\rmR^i m_*\Ql)\Rightarrow \rmH^{i+j}_c(M).$$
From it we deduce, in $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)$, a $\Lambda_A$-stable filtration whose $j$-th graded piece is
$$\bigoplus_i \rmH^j_c(N,\rmR^i m_*\Ql)=\rmH^j_c(N)\otimes \bigoplus_i \rmH^i(A).$$
These graded pieces are free $\Lambda_A$-modules. It follows that the direct sum $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)[-n]$ is a free $\Lambda_A$-module.
\end{proof}

\begin{numero}\label{relevement homologique}
Let $P$ be a smooth connected commutative algebraic group over an algebraically closed field $\bar k$. By Chevalley's theorem \cf \cite{Ros}, $P$ admits a d\'evissage as an extension of an abelian variety by an affine group
$$1\rightarrow R\rightarrow P\rightarrow A\rightarrow 1.$$
This exact sequence also exists if $P$ is defined over a perfect field, in particular over a finite field.

From the exact sequence above we deduce an exact sequence of Tate modules
$$0\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(R)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A)\rightarrow 0.$$
We call a {\em homological lifting} a linear map
$$\lambda:\rmT_\Ql(A)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)$$
splitting this exact sequence. A homological lifting induces an algebra homomorphism $\lambda:\Lambda_A\rightarrow \Lambda_P$. The set of homological liftings of the abelian part forms an affine space, a torsor under the $\Ql$-vector space ${\rm Hom}(\rmT_\Ql(A),\rmT_\Ql(R))$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}\label{quasi-relevement}
Let $P$ be a smooth connected commutative algebraic group defined over a finite field $k$. Let
$$1\rightarrow R\rightarrow P\rightarrow A\rightarrow 1$$
be the canonical exact sequence realizing $P$ as an extension of an abelian variety $A$ by an affine group $R$. Then there exist an integer $N>0$ and a homomorphism $a:A\rightarrow P$ such that composing it with $P\rightarrow A$ yields multiplication by $N$ in $A$. We then say that $a$ is a {\em quasi-lifting}.

Moreover, if $\rmT_\Ql(a):\rmT_\Ql(A)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)$ is the map induced on Tate modules, then $N^{-1}\rmT_\Ql(a)$ is the unique homological lifting compatible with the action of $\Gal(\bar k/k)$ on $\rmT(P)$ and $\rmT(A)$. We say that $N^{-1}\rmT_\Ql(a)$ is the {\em canonical lifting}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \cite[p.~184]{Serre-GACC}, the group of extensions of an abelian variety $A$ by $\GG_m$ is identified with the group of $k$-points of the dual abelian variety. The group of extensions of an abelian variety by $\GG_a$ is the finite-dimensional $k$-vector space $\rmH^1(A,\calO_A)$. A smooth connected commutative affine group is isomorphic to a product of copies of $\GG_m$ and $\GG_a$ after passing to a finite extension of the base field; it follows that the group of extensions of an abelian variety $A$ by a smooth commutative affine group $R$ defined over a finite field is a finite group. The first assertion follows.

The homological lifting $N^{-1}\rmT_\Ql(a)$ is invariant under the action of $\Gal(\bar k/k)$, since the lifting $a$ is defined over $k$. The Frobenius element $\sigma\in\Gal(\bar k/k)$ acts on $\rmT_\Ql(A)$ with eigenvalues of absolute value $|k|^{-1/2}$, and on $\rmT_\Ql(R)$ with eigenvalues of absolute value $|k|^{-1}$. Hence $N^{-1}\rmT_\Ql(a)$ is the unique homological lifting compatible with the action of $\Gal(\bar k/k)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{liberte quasi-relevement}
Let $\bar k$ be an algebraically closed field. Let $M$ be a $\bar k$-scheme of finite type. Let $P$ be a smooth connected commutative $\bar k$-group scheme acting on $M$ with affine stabilizers. Let $A$ be the maximal abelian quotient of $P$. Suppose that $P$ is defined over a finite field, so that we have a quasi-lifting $a:A\rightarrow P$. Then the $\Lambda_A$-module deduced from the $\Lambda_P$-module $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)$ is a free module.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The quasi-lifting $a:A\rightarrow P$ defines an action of $A$ on $M$ with finite stabilizers. We are thus immediately reduced to Proposition \ref{liberte ponctuel}.
\end{proof}

Here is a more general and more satisfactory statement. The proof that follows is due to Deligne \cf \cite{Deligne lettre}. Note that this statement will not be used in what follows; in fact, Lemma \ref{liberte sans point base} will play the role of the independence of the lifting in the proof of \ref{liberte}.

\begin{proposition}\label{liberte relevement arbitraire}
Let $\bar k$ be an algebraically closed field. Let $M$ be a projective $\bar k$-scheme. Let $P$ be a smooth connected commutative $\bar k$-group acting on $M$ with affine stabilizers. Let $A$ be the maximal abelian quotient of $P$ and let $\lambda:\rmT_\Ql(A)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)$ be an arbitrary homological lifting. Then the $\Lambda_A$-module deduced from the $\Lambda_P$-module $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)$ by means of $\lambda$ is a free module.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By the general process of passing to the inductive limit as in \cite[6.1.7]{BBD}, we reduce to the case where $M$, $P$ and the action of $P$ on $M$ are defined over a finite field $k$. Write $\bar k$ as an inductive limit $(A_i)_{i\in I}$ of rings of finite type over $\ZZ$. The category of $\bar k$-schemes of finite type is a $2$-inductive limit of the categories of $A_i$-schemes of finite type; that is, for every $X/\bar k$ there exist $i\in I$ and $X_i/A_i$ of finite type such that $X=X_i\otimes_{A_i}\bar k$ \cf \cite[8.9.1]{EGA4}, and for all $X_i,Y_i/A_i$ one has \cf \cite[8.8.2]{EGA4}
$${\rm Hom}_{\bar k}(X_i\otimes_{A_i}\bar k,Y_i\otimes_{A_i}\bar k)=\limind_{j\geq i} {\rm Hom}_{A_j}(X_i\otimes_{A_i}A_j,Y_i\otimes_{A_i}A_j).$$
The same statement therefore holds for the category of algebraic groups and for that of triples consisting of an algebraic group $G$, a scheme $X$ of finite type and an action of $G$ on $X$. Let $f:X\rightarrow Y$ be a morphism in the category of $\bar k$-schemes of finite type coming from a morphism $f_i:X_i\rightarrow Y_i$ in the category of $A_i$-schemes. In order for $f$ to have one of the following properties --- flat, smooth, affine or projective --- it is necessary and sufficient that $f_i$ acquire this property after an extension of scalars $A_i\rightarrow A_j$ \cf \cite[11.2.6 and 8.10.5]{EGA4}. There therefore exist a ring $A_i$ of finite type over $\ZZ$ contained in $\bar k$, a projective $A_i$-scheme $M_i$, and a smooth $A_i$-group scheme $P_i$, an extension of an abelian scheme by an affine group scheme, acting on $M_i$ with affine stabilizers, such that after the extension of scalars $A_i\rightarrow \bar k$ we recover the initial situation. We may also assume that the $\rmH^n(f_{i!}\Ql)$ are local systems. We have thus reduced the statement to be proved to the case where the base is the spectrum of a finite field.

We may therefore assume that $M$, $P$ and the action of $P$ on $M$ are defined over a finite field $k$. Let $A$ be the abelian quotient of $P$ and $a:A\rightarrow P$ the quasi-lifting, which defines a canonical lifting $\lambda_0:\rmT_\Ql(A)\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P)$ of Tate modules. We already know, by the preceding corollary \ref{liberte quasi-relevement}, that the $\Lambda_A$-module deduced from the $\Lambda_P$-module $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)$ by means of $\lambda_0$ is a free module.

Let us now prove the same statement for an arbitrary homological lifting. The space of all homological liftings, pointed by $\lambda_0$, is identified with the $\Ql$-vector space ${\rm Hom}(\rmT_\Ql(A_s),\rmT_\Ql(R_s))$, on which the Frobenius element $\sigma\in\Gal(\bar k/k)$ acts with eigenvalues of absolute value $|k|^{1/2}$. On this space, the set of those $\lambda$ for which the $\Lambda_{A_s}$-module structure on $\bigoplus_n \rmH^n_c(M)$ deduced from $\lambda$ is free forms a Zariski open subset of ${\rm Hom}(\rmT_\Ql(A_s),\rmT_\Ql(R_s))$. This open subset is stable under $\sigma$ and contains the origin $\lambda_0$. Its complement is a closed subset, stable under $\sigma$, which does not contain $\lambda_0$. Since $\sigma$ acts with eigenvalues of absolute value $|k|^{1/2}$, a closed subset of ${\rm Hom}(\rmT_\Ql(A_s),\rmT_\Ql(R_s))$ stable under $\sigma$ is necessarily stable under the action of $\GG_m$. The fact that this closed subset does not contain the origin $\lambda_0$ implies that it is empty.
\end{proof}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Study over a Henselian Base}\label{subsection : base henselienne}

In this paragraph we shall study the cap-product over a henselian base and analyse the spectral sequence abutting to the cohomology of the special fiber.

\begin{numero}\label{cap-produit henselien}
Let now $S$ be a strictly henselian scheme with a morphism $\epsilon:S\rightarrow \Spec(\bar k)$, where $\bar k$ is the residue field of $S$. Write $s$ for the closed point of $S$. The fiber $\Lambda_{P,s}$ is then identified with $\epsilon^*\Lambda_P$. By adjunction we deduce a morphism
$$\epsilon^*\Lambda_{P,s}\rightarrow \Lambda_P.$$
Restricting the cap-product \ref{cap-produit} to $\epsilon^*\Lambda_{P,s}$, we obtain an arrow
$$\Lambda_{P,s}\otimes f_!\Ql \rightarrow f_!\Ql,$$
which defines an action of the graded algebra $\Lambda_{P,s}=\bigoplus_i \wedge^i\rmT_\Ql(P_s)[i]$ on the complex $f_!\Ql$.

In particular we have a morphism
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_s)\otimes f_!\Ql \rightarrow f_!\Ql[-1].$$
From it we deduce a morphism between the truncations for the perverse $t$-structure
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_s)\otimes {}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql) \rightarrow {}^p\tau^{\leq n-1}(f_!\Ql)$$
for every $n\in\ZZ$. This induces a morphism between the perverse cohomology sheaves in degrees $n$ and $n-1$
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_s)\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql) \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_!\Ql),$$
whence an action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$ on the direct sum $\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{compatibilite cap-produit}
This arrow is compatible with the one constructed at the end of \ref{cap-produit module de Tate}. With respect to the decomposition by the support
$${}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K^n_\alpha,$$
it is expressed in terms of a matrix whose entry indexed by a pair $(\alpha,\alpha')$ is an element of
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_s)^*\otimes {\rm Hom}(K^n_\alpha,K^{n-1}_{\alpha'}).$$
The diagonal arrows are compatible with those of \ref{fleche diagonale}, whereas the non-diagonal arrows are here clearly zero, since ${\rm Hom}(K^n_\alpha,K^{n-1}_{\alpha'})=0$ if $\alpha\not=\alpha'$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{suite spectrale}
The filtration ${}^p\tau^{\leq n}(f_!\Ql)$ of $f_!\Ql$ defines a spectral sequence
$$E^2_{m,n}=\rmH^m({}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)_s)\Rightarrow \rmH^{m+n}_c(M_s)$$
which is equivariant for the action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$. This spectral sequence is convergent because the complex $f_!\Ql$ is bounded. We therefore have, on the direct sum
$$\rmH^\bullet_c(M_s)=\bigoplus_r \rmH^r_c(M_s)[-r],$$
a decreasing filtration ${\rm F}^m\rmH_c(M_s)$ such that
$${\rm F}^m\rmH_c(M_s)/{\rm F}^{m+1}\rmH_c(M_s)=\bigoplus_n E^\infty_{m,n}[-m-n].$$
The action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$ on $\rmH^\bullet_c(M_s)$ respects this filtration. The induced action on the graded pieces associated with the filtration ${\rm F}^m\rmH_c(M_s)$
$$\bigoplus_n E^\infty_{m,n}[-m-n]$$
is deduced from its action on the $E^2_{m,n}$, which in turn is deduced from the graded action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$ on $\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)[-n]$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{degenerescence E2}
Let us now place ourselves under the hypotheses of \ref{inegalite delta}. In particular we have $f_!=f_*$. Having purity at our disposal as well, there exists a non-canonical isomorphism over $S\otimes_k\bar k$
$$f_*\Ql \simeq \bigoplus_{n\in\ZZ} {}^p\rmH^n(f_!\Ql)[-n].$$
This isomorphism persists when restricted to the strict henselization $S_s$ of a geometric point $s$ of $S$, so that the spectral sequences \ref{suite spectrale} degenerate at $E^2$, that is, $E^\infty_{m,n}=E^2_{m,n}$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{pas de decomposition compatible}
At the level of the perverse cohomology sheaves we have a canonical decomposition by the support
$${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K^n_\alpha.$$
We observed in \ref{compatibilite cap-produit} that over a strictly henselian base the action of the graded algebra $\Lambda_{P,s}$ on the direct sum $\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ respects the decomposition according to the support. It is very tempting to look for an isomorphism
$$f_*\Ql \isom \bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$$
better than the others, which would in particular be compatible with the action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$ defined in \ref{cap-produit henselien}. In that case one could use the freeness of the abutment of the spectral sequence to conclude the proof of \ref{liberte}. However, a direct sum decomposition of $f_*\Ql$ compatible with the action of $\Lambda_{P,s}$ does not appear to exist.

One can nevertheless prove \ref{liberte} by relying on the degeneration of the spectral sequence and proceeding by induction on the strata. This is what we shall do in the following paragraph.
\end{numero}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Freeness by Induction}\label{subsection : liberte recurrence}

In this paragraph we shall complete the proof of the freeness property \ref{liberte}, and hence also those of the amplitude inequality \ref{inegalite amplitude} and of the delta inequality \ref{inegalite delta}.

\begin{numero}\label{initialisation recurrence}
We prove Proposition \ref{liberte} by a descending induction on the dimension of $Z_\alpha$. Let $\alpha_0\in\FrakB$ be the maximal element, so that $Z_{\alpha_0}$ is the whole of $S\otimes_k\bar k$. Let $V_{\alpha_0}$ be a dense open subset of $S\otimes_k\bar k$ small enough in the sense of \ref{ouvert V alpha}. For $\alpha\not=\alpha_0$, the other strata $Z_\alpha$ having no intersection with $V_{\alpha_0}$, the restriction of the perverse cohomology sheaf ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ to $V_{\alpha_0}$ is a local system with a shift
$${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)|_{U_{\alpha_0}}=\calK^n_{\alpha_0}[\dim(S)].$$
Here $\calK^n_{\alpha_0}$ is a semisimple local system on $U_{\alpha_0}$, pure of weight $n$ by the purity hypothesis of \ref{inegalite delta}.

As in \ref{ouvert V alpha}, over the open subset $V_{\alpha_0}$ the sheaf of Tate modules $\rmT_\Ql(P)$ is devissed into an exact sequence
$$0\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(R_{\alpha_0})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{\alpha_0})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_{\alpha_0})\rightarrow 0,$$
where $\rmT_\Ql(A_{\alpha_0})$ is a pure local system of weight $-1$ and $\rmT_\Ql(R_{\alpha_0})$ is a pure local system of weight $-2$. As explained in \ref{structure de module gradue}, the action of the Tate module
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_{\alpha_0})\otimes \calK^n_{\alpha_0}\rightarrow \calK^{n-1}_{\alpha_0}$$
factors through $\rmT_\Ql(A_{\alpha_0})$ for reasons of weight.

The first step of the induction consists in showing that for every geometric point $u_{\alpha_0}$ of $V_{\alpha_0}$, the resulting $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha_0},u_{\alpha_0}}$-module structure on the direct sum
$$\rmH^\bullet(M_{u_{\alpha_0}}):=\bigoplus_n \rmH^n(M_{u_{\alpha_0}})[-n]=\bigoplus_n \calK^n_{\alpha_0,u_{\alpha_0}}[-n+\dim(S)]$$
makes it a free module.

By \ref{liberte sans point base}, this assertion is independent of the choice of $u_{\alpha_0}$. We may in fact assume that the geometric point $u_{\alpha_0}$ is defined over a finite field.
