As in \ref{quasi-relevement}, there then exists a quasi-lifting $A_{\alpha_0}\rightarrow P_{\alpha_0}$, so that the action of $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha_0},u_{\alpha_0}}$ on $\rmH^\bullet(M_{u_{\alpha_0}})$ comes from a geometric action of $A_{\alpha_0,u_{\alpha_0}}$ on $M_{u_{\alpha_0}}$. We now use the hypothesis that the fiber $M_{u_{\alpha_0}}$ is projective to conclude that $\rmH^\bullet(M_{u_{\alpha_0}})$ is a free module over $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha_0},u_{\alpha_0}}$, as in Lemma \ref{liberte ponctuel}.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{action henselienne recurrence}
We shall use the freeness property of the cohomology of the fiber $M_{u_\alpha}$ to deduce the freeness of the graded local system $\calK_\alpha$ as a $\Lambda_{A_\alpha}$-module. The difficulty is to control the noise caused by the $\calK_{\alpha'}$ with $\alpha'\in\FrakB$ such that $Z_{\alpha'}$ is strictly contained in $Z_\alpha$. Note that by induction we may assume that for such $\alpha'$, $\calK_{\alpha'}$ is a free module over $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha'}}$.

Take a geometric point $u_\alpha$ of $V_\alpha$ lying over a point with values in a finite field. Let $S_{u_\alpha}$ be the strict henselization of $S$ at $u_\alpha$. The construction of \ref{cap-produit henselien} applies to $S_{u_\alpha}$. We therefore have an action of $\Lambda_{P,u_\alpha}$ on the restriction of $f_*\Ql$ to $S_{u_\alpha}$
\begin{equation}\label{action henselienne}
\Lambda_{P,u_\alpha}\otimes (f_*\Ql|_{S_{u_\alpha}}) \rightarrow (f_*\Ql|_{S_{u_\alpha}}).
\end{equation}
As in \ref{cap-produit henselien}, this induces a graded action of $\Lambda_{P,u_\alpha}$ on the direct sum of perverse cohomology sheaves, whose degree $-1$ part reads
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)|_{S_{u_\alpha}} \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_*\Ql)|_{S_{u_\alpha}}.$$
By \ref{compatibilite cap-produit}, this arrow is expressed in terms of a diagonal matrix with respect to the canonical decomposition of ${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)$ and ${}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_*\Ql)$ by the support
$${}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K^n_\alpha.$$
In other words, the direct sum decomposition
$$\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\FrakB} K_\alpha$$
respects the structure of graded $\Lambda_{P,u_\alpha}$-modules.

The geometric point $u_\alpha$ being defined over a finite field, by \ref{quasi-relevement} there exists a quasi-lifting $A_{u_\alpha}\rightarrow P_{u_\alpha}$ which induces a canonical direct sum decomposition
$$\rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})=\rmT_\Ql(R_{u_\alpha})\oplus \rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha}).$$
From it we deduce an arrow
$$\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\otimes {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)|_{S_{u_\alpha}} \rightarrow {}^p\rmH^{n-1}(f_*\Ql)|_{S_{u_\alpha}}$$
which decomposes, by what precedes, into a direct sum of arrows
$$\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\otimes K^n_{\alpha'}|_{S_{u_\alpha}} \rightarrow K^{n-1}_{\alpha'}|_{S_{u_\alpha}}$$
over the set of indices $\alpha'\in\FrakB$ such that $Z_{\alpha'}$ is contained in $Z_\alpha$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}\label{liberte bruit}
For every $\alpha'\in\FrakB$ such that $Z_{\alpha'}$ is strictly contained in $Z_\alpha$, and for every integer $m$, the graded $\Ql$-vector space
$$\bigoplus_{n\in\ZZ}\rmH^m(K^n_{\alpha',u_\alpha})[-n]$$
is a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Over $V_{\alpha'}$ we have a canonical arrow between graded local systems
$$\Lambda_{A_{\alpha'}}\otimes \calK_{\alpha'}\rightarrow \calK_{\alpha'}$$
defined in \ref{structure de module gradue}. By the induction hypothesis, for every geometric point $u_{\alpha'}$ of $V_{\alpha'}$ the fiber of $\calK_{\alpha'}$ at $u_{\alpha'}$ is a free $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha'},u_{\alpha'}}$-module. By the decomposition theorem, $\calK_{\alpha'}$ is a semisimple graded local system. Applying Lemma \ref{liberte sans point base}, we know that there exist a graded local system $E_{\alpha'}$ on $V_{\alpha'}$ and an isomorphism
$$\calK_{\alpha'}\simeq \Lambda_{A_{\alpha'}}\otimes E_{\alpha'}$$
as graded $\Lambda_{A_{\alpha'}}$-modules.

This factorization continues to exist on the intersection $V_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}$. Write $y_{\alpha'}$ for the generic point of $V_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}$ and $\bar y_{\alpha'}$ for a geometric point above $y_{\alpha'}$. We then have an isomorphism of representations of $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$
$$L_{\alpha',\bar y_{\alpha'}}=\Lambda_{A_{\alpha',\bar y_{\alpha'}}}\otimes E_{\alpha',\bar y_{\alpha'}}.$$

\begin{lemma}\label{polarisation injective}
Under the hypothesis that $\rmT_\Ql(P)$ is polarizable as in \ref{module de Tate polarisable}, for every homological lifting $\beta:\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})$, the map
$$\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$$
obtained by composing $\beta$ with the specialization arrow $\rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ followed by the canonical projection $\rmT_\Ql(P_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ is injective. Moreover, there exists a complement of $\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})$ in $\rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ which is $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$-stable.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The specialization arrow $\rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ is compatible with the alternating polarization form. Any homological lifting $\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{u_\alpha})$ is compatible with the alternating form, which vanishes on the affine part $\rmT_\Ql(R_{u_\alpha})$, so that the map $\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(P_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ deduced from it is too. It follows that $\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\rightarrow \rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ is injective and that the orthogonal of $\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})$ in $\rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})$ is a $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$-stable complement.
\end{proof}

Let us continue the proof of \ref{liberte bruit}. The direct sum decomposition \ref{polarisation injective}
$$\rmT_\Ql(A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}})=\rmT_\Ql(A_{u_\alpha})\oplus \rmU$$
of representations of $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$ induces an isomorphism of representations of $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$
$$\Lambda_{A_{\bar y_{\alpha'}}}=\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}\otimes \Lambda(\rmU),$$
where $\Lambda(\rmU)=\bigoplus_i \wedge^i(\rmU)[i]$. This implies a factorization as a tensor product of representations of $\Gal(\bar y_{\alpha'}/y_{\alpha'})$
$$\calK_{\alpha',\bar y_{\alpha'}}=\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}\otimes \Lambda(\rmU)\otimes E_{\alpha',\bar y_{\alpha'}}.$$
There therefore exists an isomorphism
$$\calK_{\alpha'}|_{V_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}}=\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}\boxtimes E'_{\alpha'},$$
where $E'_{\alpha'}$ is a local system on $V_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}$. Since the external tensor product with $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$ commutes with the intermediate extension from $V_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}$ to $Z_{\alpha'}\cap S_{u_\alpha}$ and with the fiber functor at $u_{\alpha_0}$, Proposition \ref{liberte bruit} follows.
\end{proof}

Consider now the spectral sequence \ref{suite spectrale}
$$E_2^{m,n}=\rmH^m({}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)_{u_\alpha})\Rightarrow \rmH^{m+n}(M_{u_\alpha}),$$
which degenerates at $E_2$ by \ref{degenerescence E2}. We thus obtain a filtration of
$$\rmH=\bigoplus_j \rmH^j(M_{u_\alpha})[-j]$$
whose $m$-th graded piece is
$$\rmH^m\Big(\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)_{s_0}[-n]\Big)[-m].$$
This filtration is stable under the action of $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$. Its action on the $m$-th graded piece is deduced from the action of $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$ on the direct sum
$$\bigoplus_n {}^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql)[-n]_{S_{u_\alpha}}$$
and hence from that on the $K_{\alpha'}|_{S_{u_\alpha}}$. This $m$-th graded piece therefore decomposes into a direct sum of graded $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-modules
$$\rmH^m\Big(\bigoplus_{\alpha'}\bigoplus_{n\in\ZZ} K^n_{\alpha',u_\alpha}[-n]\Big)[-m].$$

For $\alpha'\not=\alpha$, we already know that $\rmH^m(\bigoplus_{n\in\ZZ} K^n_{\alpha',u_\alpha}[-n])$ is a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module \cf \ref{liberte bruit}. For $\alpha'=\alpha$, we have $\rmH^m(K_{\alpha,u_\alpha})=0$ except for $m=-\dim(Z_\alpha)$. We thus obtain a filtration of $\rmH$ by $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-submodules
$$0\subset \rmH'\subset \rmH''\subset \rmH=\bigoplus_j \rmH^j(M_{u_\alpha})$$
such that $\rmH'$ and $\rmH/\rmH''$ are free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-modules and such that
$$\rmH''/\rmH'=L_{\alpha,u_\alpha}.$$
By \ref{liberte quasi-relevement}, we know that $\rmH$ is also a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module. We shall deduce from this that $L_{\alpha,u_\alpha}$ is also a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module, by means of a particular property of the ring $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$.

Since $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$ is a local algebra, every projective $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module is free. The exact sequence
$$0\rightarrow \rmH''\rightarrow \rmH\rightarrow \rmH/\rmH''\rightarrow 0,$$
with $\rmH$ and $\rmH/\rmH''$ free, implies that $\rmH''$ is also free.

Note also that $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$ is a local $\Ql$-algebra of finite dimension having a socle of dimension one. From this we deduce that the dual $(\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}})^*$ of $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$ as a $\Ql$-vector space is a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module. Consider the dual exact sequence
$$0\rightarrow (\rmH''/\rmH')^*\rightarrow (\rmH'')^*\rightarrow (\rmH')^*\rightarrow 0,$$
where $(\rmH'')^*$ and $(\rmH')^*$ are free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-modules. It follows that $(\rmH''/\rmH')^*$ is a free $\Lambda_{A_{u_\alpha}}$-module, so that $\rmH''/\rmH'$ is one too.
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{The Case of the Hitchin Fibration}\label{subsection : description support}

Let us apply the result of this section to the case of the Hitchin fibration, more precisely to the morphism $\tilde f^\ani:\tilde\calM^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$ equipped with the action of $\tilde g^\ani:\tilde\calP^\ani\rightarrow\tilde\calA^\ani$. Here $\tilde\calM^\ani$ and $\tilde\calP^\ani$ are not schemes but Deligne-Mumford stacks of finite type \cf \ref{propre}. We know that $\tilde\calP^\ani$ is smooth over $\tilde\calA^\ani$ by \ref{P lisse} and that $\tilde\calM^\ani$ is smooth over $k$ by \ref{lisse}. By \ref{Ma projectif}, the action of $\tilde\calP^\ani$ on $\tilde\calM^\ani$ has affine stabilizers. By \ref{densite globale}, the morphism $\tilde f^\ani$ is flat of relative dimension $d$, and the same holds for $\tilde g^\ani$. By \ref{pi}, the sheaf $\pi_0(\tilde\calP^\ani)$ is a finite quotient of the constant sheaf $\bbX_*$. The Tate module is polarizable by \ref{T ell P est polarisable}. The morphism $\tilde f^\ani$ is proper without being projective; nevertheless $\tilde\calM^\ani$ is homeomorphic to a projective scheme $\bar\calM^\ani$ over $\tilde\calA^\ani$ by \ref{propre}.

There are two ways of applying the inequality \ref{support general kappa} to our situation. The first is to replace $\tilde\calM^\ani$ by $\bar\calM^\ani$, which is projective over $\tilde\calA^\ani$. The constant sheaf $\Ql$ on $\bar\calM^\ani$ is pure, because the stack $\tilde\calM^\ani$, which is homeomorphic to it, is smooth. One may replace the Deligne-Mumford Picard stack $\tilde\calP^\ani$ by the group scheme $\calP^{\infty,\ani}$ defined by means of a rigidifier \ref{rigidificateur}. Here $\calP^{\infty,\ani}$ has relative dimension larger than $d$, but adapting \ref{support general kappa} to this situation is merely a matter of numerology.

In fact, there is a much more economical way to proceed. It consists in observing that the entire proof of \ref{support general kappa} adapts as it stands to Deligne-Mumford stacks, with the exception of Lemma \ref{liberte ponctuel}, where we assumed that the fiber $\calM_a$ is projective. But by \ref{Ma projectif} we know that $\calM_a$ is homeomorphic to a projective scheme, so that \ref{support general kappa} applies. Note also that instead of using \ref{Ma projectif} and \ref{liberte ponctuel} in combination, it is even more economical to apply the product formula \ref{produit} directly in order to obtain the conclusion of \ref{liberte ponctuel}. In the situation of the Hitchin fibration, where a product formula is available, paragraph \ref{subsection : liberte ponctuel} is in fact not necessary.

\begin{numero}\label{conjecture delta regularite}
We have a stratification \cf \ref{stratification delta ani}
$$\tilde\calA^\ani=\bigsqcup_{\delta\in\NN}\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta$$
such that for every $a\in\calA^\ani_\delta(\bar k)$ the dimension of the affine part of $\calP_a$ equals $\delta$. We conjecture that for every $\delta$ we have
$${\rm codim}(\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta)\geq \delta.$$
The strongest evidence in favour of this conjecture is the case of characteristic zero, a proof of which is sketched in \cite[p.~4]{N-preparation}. Failing a proof of this conjecture in characteristic $p$, we must resort to a stratagem.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}\label{delta bad}
Suppose that there exist integers $\delta$ such that ${\rm codim}(\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta)<\delta$; we shall write $\delta_G^{\rm bad}(D)$ for the smallest of them. It is important to note the dependence of $\delta_G^{\rm bad}(D)$, if it exists, on $G$ and on the invertible bundle $D$. By \ref{codimension}, we know that for a fixed group $G$ the integer $\delta_G^{\rm bad}(D)$ tends to infinity with $\deg(D)$. We write
$$\tilde\calA^{\rm bad}=\bigsqcup_{\delta\geq \delta_G^{\rm bad}(D)}\tilde\calA^\ani_\delta$$
and
$$\tilde\calA^{\rm good}=\tilde\calA^\ani-\tilde\calA^{\rm bad}.$$
By construction, over $\tilde\calA^{\rm good}$ the morphisms $\tilde f^\ani$ and $\tilde g^\ani$ form a $\delta$-regular abelian fibration.
\end{numero}

\begin{theoreme}\label{support stable}
Let ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_{\rm st}$ be the largest direct factor of ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)$ on which $\bbX_*$ acts trivially. Let $K$ be a geometrically irreducible perverse factor of ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_{\rm st}$ and let $Z$ be the support of $K$. Suppose that $Z\cap\tilde\calA^{\rm good}\not=\emptyset$. Then $Z=\tilde\calA^\ani$.
\end{theoreme}

\begin{proof}
Since $\tilde\calP$ is $\delta$-regular over $\tilde\calA^{\rm good}$, we have the inequality ${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_Z$. Applying \ref{support general kappa} with trivial $\kappa$, we obtain on the one hand the equality ${\rm codim}(Z)=\delta_Z$, and on the other hand the existence of an open subset $U$ of $\tilde\calA^\ani$ and a local system $L$ on $U\cap Z$ such that $i_*L$, where $i$ is the closed immersion $Z\cap U\rightarrow U$, is a direct factor of $\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)_{\rm st}|_U$. But we know by \ref{cohomologie ordinaire max} that the latter is a constant sheaf on $\tilde\calA^\ani$, so that $U\cap Z=U$ and therefore $Z=\tilde\calA^\ani$.
\end{proof}

\begin{numero}\label{delta bad H}
The application of \ref{support general kappa} to the $\kappa$-part is more subtle, because a priori it is possible that $\tilde\calA^{\rm good}\cap\tilde\calA_\kappa=\emptyset$. Indeed, $\tilde\calA_\kappa$ is the union of the $\tilde\calA_H$, where $H$ runs over the endoscopic groups associated with the pointed endoscopic data $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$, and the codimension of $\tilde\calA_H$ in $\tilde\calA$ is a multiple of $\deg(D)$. Nevertheless, it is not necessary to know that the abelian fibration is $\delta$-regular in order to apply the inequalities \ref{inegalite delta} and \ref{support general kappa}; it in fact suffices to know the inequality ${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_Z$ for the possible supports $Z$.

Replacing $G$ by an endoscopic group $H$, we have an integer $\delta_H^{\rm bad}(D)$, a closed subscheme
$$\tilde\calA_H^{\rm bad}=\bigsqcup_{\delta_H\geq \delta_H^{\rm bad}(D)}\tilde\calA^\ani_{\delta_H}$$
and its complementary open subset $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}=\tilde\calA_H-\tilde\calA_H^{\rm bad}$.
\end{numero}

\begin{theoreme}\label{support faible}
Let ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ be the largest direct factor of ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)$ on which $\bbX_*$ acts through the character $\kappa$. Let $K$ be a geometrically irreducible perverse factor of ${}^p\rmH^n(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$ and let $Z$ be the support of $K$. Then there exists a pointed endoscopic datum $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$ such that $Z$ is contained in $\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)$, where $H$ is the endoscopic group attached to $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$ and $\tilde\nu:\tilde\calA_H\rightarrow\tilde\calA$ is the closed immersion deduced from it.

If moreover $Z\cap\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good})\not=\emptyset$, then $Z=\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H^\ani)$.
\end{theoreme}

\begin{proof}
We know that $Z\subset\tilde\calA_\kappa$, where \cf \ref{tilde A_kappa}
$$\tilde\calA_\kappa=\bigcup_{(\kappa,\rho^\bullet_{\kappa,\xi})}\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_{H_\xi}),$$
so that the irreducible closed subset $Z$ is contained in one of the irreducible components $\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_{H_\xi})$. We shall simply write $H$ for the endoscopic group attached to the endoscopic datum $(\kappa,\rho_\kappa^\bullet)$ such that $Z\subset\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)$. Let $Z_H$ be the closed subset of $\tilde\calA_H$ such that $Z=\tilde\nu(Z_H)$.

Suppose that $Z_H\cap\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}\not=\emptyset$; then we have the inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z_H)\geq \delta_{H,Z_H},$$
where $\delta_H$ is the delta function for the Picard stack $\tilde\calP_H$ over $\tilde\calA_H$. By \ref{delta-dellta_H} and \ref{codim A_H}, we know that for every $a_H\in\tilde\calA_H^\ani(\bar k)$ the difference
$$\delta(\tilde\nu(a_H))-\delta_H(a_H)$$
is independent of $a_H$ and equals exactly the codimension of $\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)$ in $\tilde\calA$. We therefore have
$${\rm codim}(Z)\geq \delta_H(a_H)+{\rm codim}(\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H))=\delta_Z.$$

Applying \ref{support general kappa}, there exist an open subset $U$ of $\tilde\calA^\ani$ and a non-trivial local system $L$ on $Z\cap U$ such that $i_*L$, where $i$ is the closed immersion $i:Z\cap U\rightarrow U$, is a direct factor of $\rmH^{2d}(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_\kappa$. By \ref{cohomologie ordinaire max}, we then know that $Z\cap U=\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)\cap U$, so that finally $Z=\tilde\nu(\tilde\calA_H)$.
\end{proof}

\section{Point Counting}
\label{section : comptage}

In this last chapter, we complete the proof of the geometric stabilization theorem \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A} as well as that of the Langlands-Shelstad conjectures \ref{LS} and Waldspurger \ref{Waldspurger} by relying on theorem \ref{support faible}.

The proof is based on the following principle. Let $K_1,K_2$ be two pure complexes on an irreducible $k$-scheme of finite type $S$. Suppose that every geometrically simple perverse sheaf present in $K_1$ or $K_2$ has support $S$ entirely, then to demonstrate that $K_1$ and $K_2$ have the same class in the Grothendieck group, it suffices to demonstrate that there exists a dense open set $U$ of $S$ such that for any finite extension $k'$ of $k$, for any $u\in U(k')$, the traces of the Frobenius $\sigma_{k'}$ of $k'$ on $K_{1,u}$ and $K_{2,u}$ are equal. The equality in the Grothendieck group implies that we have the same equality but for every point $s\in S(k')$. Point counting in a fiber above a point of a small open set $U$ should be much more agreeable than in any fiber, which is the strength of the support theorem.

We will therefore count the number $\sharp \calM_a(k)$ of $k$-points in an anisotropic Hitchin fiber.
More precisely, we want a formula for the stable part $\sharp \calM_a(k)_{\rm st}$.
The product formula \ref{produit} allows us to express this number as a product of the number $\sharp\calP_a^0(k)$ of $k$-points of the neutral component of $\calP_a$ and the numbers of points in quotients of affine Springer fibers \cf \ref{subsection : comptage Hitchin}. For these quotients of affine Springer fibers, a more or less direct counting yields a stable orbital integral \cf \ref{subsection : comptage Springer}. We also have the counting variant with a $\kappa$-weighting which gives rise to $\kappa$-orbital integrals. Each time, it is a matter of counting points of an Artin stack of the form $[M/P]$ where $M$ is a $k$-scheme and $P$ is a $k$-algebraic group acting on $M$. This formalism is recalled in \ref{subsection : comptage} along with an ad hoc fixed-point formula which was proved in appendix A.3 of \cite{LN}.

For $a\in \calA^\diamondsuit(k)$, the quotients of affine Springer fibers are all trivial such that $\sharp \calM_a(k)_{\rm st}$ is equal to the number $\sharp \calP_a^0(k)$ where $\calP_a^0$ is essentially an abelian variety. This allows us to prove the equality of the numbers $\sharp \calM_{1,a}(k)_{\rm st}$ and $\sharp \calM_{2,a}(k)_{\rm st}$ for $a\in\calA^\diamondsuit(k)$ associated with two groups $G_1$ and $G_2$ having isogenous root data. The support theorem \ref{support stable} allows extending the identity to $a\in (\calA^{\rm ani}-\calA^{\rm bad})(k)$. We thus obtain enough global points to obtain all local stable orbital integrals by letting $\deg(D)$ tend to infinity. We thus prove the non-standard fundamental lemma conjectured by Waldspurger \cf \ref{subsection : demo Waldspurger}. By reversing the global-local argument, we can extend the identity $\sharp \calM_{1,a}(k)_{\rm st}=\sharp \calM_{2,a}(k)_{\rm st}$ to all $a\in\calA^\ani(k)$.

The proof of the Langlands-Shelstad conjecture essentially follows the same strategy with a few more technical difficulties. For $a_H\in\tilde\calA_H^\diamondsuit(k)$, the local stable orbital integrals of $a_H$ are trivial but the local $\kappa$-orbital integrals in $G$ of the corresponding point $a$ are not necessarily so. However, by shrinking $\tilde\calA_H^\diamondsuit$ even further, we can assume that these non-trivial local $\kappa$-orbital integrals are as simple as possible. The calculation of these simple local integrals is well known and reduces to the ${\rm SL}(2)$ case treated by Labesse and Langlands. We review it in \ref{subsection : fibre simple}. By combining this calculation with the support theorem \ref{support faible}, we obtain the part of the geometric stabilization theorem on the open set $\tilde\calA^\ani_H-\tilde\calA^{\rm bad}_H$ \cf \ref{subsection : A good}. Again, by letting $\deg(D)$ tend to infinity, we obtain all local orbital integrals and thus prove the fundamental lemma \cf \ref{lemme fondamental}. By reversing the global-local argument, we deduce the entire geometric stabilization theorem.

\subsection{General remarks on counting}\label{subsection : comptage}

In this paragraph, we will fix a framework for the various
counting problems that we will have to solve later. We
will also clarify the abuses of notation that we will
systematically practice in this chapter. In this
paragraph and unlike the rest of the article, the letter $X$ does not necessarily designate
a curve.

\begin{numero}
If $M$ is a $k$-scheme of finite type. According to the
Grothendieck-Lefschetz trace formula, the number of $k$-points of $M$ can be
calculated as an alternating sum of traces of the Frobenius
element $\sigma\in\Gal(\bar k/ k)$
$${\sharp\,} M(k)=\sum_n (-1)^n \tr(\sigma,\rmH^n_c(M))$$
where $\rmH^n_c(M)$ denotes the $n$-th compactly supported cohomology group
$\rmH^n_c(M\otimes_k \bar k,\Ql)$ of $M\otimes_k \bar k$.
\end{numero}

We will need a variant of this trace formula in the
following context. Let $M$ be a $k$-scheme of finite type or more
generally a Deligne-Mumford stack of finite type, equipped with an
action of a commutative $k$-algebraic group of finite type $P$. We
want to relate the trace of $\sigma$ on a part of the compactly supported
cohomology of $M$ with the number of points of the quotient $X=[M/ P]$ and the
number of points of the neutral component of $P$. This is the content of
appendix A.3 of \cite{LN} which we will now recall and
slightly generalize.
\begin{numero}
Let $X=[M/ P]$ be as above. We will write
$X$ for the groupoid $X(\bar k)$, $M$ for the set $M(\bar k)$ and $P$
for the group $P(\bar k)$. By definition, the set of objects of $X=[M/
P]$ is the set $M$. Let $m_1, m_2\in M$. Then the set of
morphisms $\Hom_X(m_1,m_2)$ is the transporter
$$\Hom_X(m_1,m_2)=\{p\in P| pm_1=m_2\}.$$
The composition rule for morphisms is deduced from multiplication in
the group $P$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
The action of the Frobenius element $\sigma\in \Gal(\bar k/ k)$ on $M$ and
$P$ induces an action on the groupoid $X=[M/ P]$. The groupoid $X(k)$ of
fixed points under the action of $\sigma$ is by definition the category whose
\begin{itemize}
\item objects are pairs $(m,p)$ where $m\in M$ and $p\in P$ such
  that $p\sigma(m)=m$;
\item a morphism $h:(m,p)\rightarrow (m',p')$ in $X(k)$ is an
  element $h\in P$ such that $hm=m'$ and $p'=hp\sigma(h)^{-1}$.
\end{itemize}
The category $X(k)$ has a finite number of isomorphism classes of objects and
each object has a finite number of automorphisms. We are interested in the
sum
$${\sharp\,} X(k)=\sum_{x\in X(k)/\sim} \frac{1}{{\sharp\,} \Aut_{X(k)}(x)}$$
where $x$ ranges over a set of representatives of the
isomorphism classes of objects in $X(k)$.
\end{numero}


\begin{numero}
Let $X=[M/ P]$ as above and let $x=(m,p)$ be an object of $X(k)$. By
definition of the morphisms in $X(k)$, the $\sigma$-conjugacy class of
$p$ depends only on the isomorphism class of $x$. Since $P$ is a
$k$-group of finite type, the group of $\sigma$-conjugacy classes of
$P$ is canonically identified with $\rmH^1(k,P)$. Let $\cl(x)\in
\rmH^1(k,P)$ denote the $\sigma$-conjugacy class of $p$ which depends only
on the isomorphism class of $x$. According to a theorem of Lang,
$\rmH^1(k,P)$ is identified with $\rmH^1(k,\pi_0(P))$ where $\pi_0(P)$ denotes
the group of connected components of $P\otimes_k
\bar k$ which is thus a finite commutative group equipped with an action of
$\sigma$. Thus, for any $\sigma$-invariant character $\kappa:
\pi_0(P)\rightarrow \Ql^\times$, for any object $x\in X(k)$, we can
define a pairing
$$\langle \cl(x), \kappa \rangle =\kappa(\cl(x)) \in \Ql^\times$$
which depends only on the isomorphism class of $x$. We can therefore
define the number of points of $X(k)$ with $\kappa$-weighting
$${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa= \sum_{x\in X(k)/\sim}
\frac {\langle \cl(x), \kappa \rangle}{{\sharp\,} \Aut_{X(k)}(x)}$$
where $x$ ranges over a set of representatives of the
isomorphism classes of objects in $X(k)$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
The group $P$ acts on the cohomology groups $\rmH^n_c(M)$
through its group of connected components $\pi_0(P)$ according to the
homotopy lemma. We can form the eigenspace
$\rmH^n_c(M)_\kappa$ of $\rmH^n_c(M)$ with eigenvalue $\kappa$.
Since $\kappa$ is $\sigma$-invariant, $\sigma$ acts on
$\rmH^n_c(M)_\kappa$.
\end{numero}

We have the following variant of the Grothendieck-Lefschetz trace formula
proved in appendix A.3 of \cite{LN}.

\begin{proposition}\label{formule des points fixes}
% [journal 8.1.6] the commutativity hypothesis on P was dropped, although the whole
% statement (characters of pi_0(P), the kappa-weighting) depends on it.
Let $M$ be a $k$-scheme of finite type, $P$ a commutative $k$-group of finite type
acting on $M$ and $X=[M/ P]$. Then the category $X(k)$ has a finite number
of isomorphism classes of objects and each object has a finite number
of automorphisms. For any $\kappa:\pi_0(P)\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ a
$\sigma$-invariant character, the number ${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa$ has
the following cohomological interpretation:
$${\sharp\,} P^0(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa= \sum_n (-1)^n\tr(\sigma,\rmH^n_c(M)_\kappa)$$
where $P^0$ is the neutral component of $P$.
\end{proposition}

% [journal 8.1.7 - 8.1.15] MISPLACED IN THE arXiv VERSION: this block (the two
% classifying-stack examples, the finiteness hypothesis, the kappa-descent lemma, the
% fixed-point formula 8.1.13, its corollary and the G_m example) sat at the END of
% "Counting in an affine Springer fiber". In the journal it belongs to 8.1, directly
% after Proposition 8.1.6, which is what its opening sentence refers to. Moved back.


We refer to \cite[A.3.1]{LN} for the proof. We can
consider two instructive examples that explain why we need to
separate the role of $P^0$ from $\pi_0(P)$.

\begin{exemple}\label{exemple : classifiant fini}
Suppose $M=\Spec(k)$ and $P$ is a finite group on which
$\sigma$ acts. Let $X=[M/ P]$ be the classifying stack of $P$. Then the objects of the
category $X(k)$ are the elements $p\in P$ while the morphisms
$p\rightarrow p'$ are the elements $h\in P$ such that
$p'=hp\sigma(h)^{-1}$. We then have
$${\sharp\,} X(k)=\frac{{\sharp\,} P} {{\sharp\,} P}=1.$$
Furthermore, for any $\sigma$-invariant character $\kappa:
P\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ which is non-trivial, we have ${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=0$.
\end{exemple}

\begin{exemple}\label{exemple : classifiant G_m}
If $M=\Spec(k)$ and $P=\GG_m$. Let $X=[M/ P]$ be the classifying stack of $\GG_m$.
The objects of $X(k)$ are again the elements $p\in P$ while the
morphisms $p\rightarrow p'$ are the elements $h\in P$ such that
$p'=hp\sigma(h)^{-1}$. According to Lang, all elements of $\bar
k^\times$ are $\sigma$-conjugate such that there is only one
isomorphism class of objects in $X(k)$. The automorphism group of
each object in $X(k)$ is $k^\times$ and thus has $q-1$ elements. We have
therefore ${\sharp\,} X(k)=(q-1)^{-1}$.
\end{exemple}

To study point counting in affine Springer fibers,
we need a variant of the previous discussion for the case where
$M$ and $P$ are locally of finite type and where $[M/ P]$ has a
reasonable finiteness property.

Let $M$ be a locally finite type $k$-scheme and $P$ a locally finite type
commutative $k$-group acting on $M$. For the quotient
$[M/ P]$ to be reasonably finite, we also make the following hypotheses.

\begin{hypothese}\label{hypothese : finitude}
\begin{enumerate}
 \item The group of connected components $\pi_0(P)$ is a
   % [journal 8.1.9(1)] "de type fini" = FINITELY GENERATED, not finite. Items (2) and (3)
   % of this same list already render it as "of finite type"; 8.1.10 builds the maximal
   % free quotient pi_0(P)^lib, which is vacuous if pi_0(P) were finite.
   finitely generated abelian group.
 \item The stabilizer in $P$ of each point of $M$ is a
   subgroup of finite type.
 \item There exists a discrete torsion-free subgroup $\Lambda \subset P$
   such that $P/ \Lambda$ and $M/ \Lambda$ are of finite type.
\end{enumerate}
\end{hypothese}

The last hypothesis needs to be commented on. Since the
group $\Lambda$ is torsion-free, it acts without fixed points on $M$ since
the stabilizers in $P$ of any point of $M$ is of finite type
and in particular have a trivial intersection with $\Lambda$. Moreover, if
the hypothesis is verified, it is verified for any
subgroup $\Lambda'\subset \Lambda$ of finite index and $\sigma$-invariant.

\begin{numero}
We also need to explain how to construct a discrete torsion-free subgroup
$\Lambda$ of $P$ such that $P/ \Lambda$ is of finite type. The group
$P$ admits a canonical short exact sequence
$$1\rightarrow P^{\rm tf} \rightarrow P \rightarrow \pi_0(P)^{\rm lib} \rightarrow 0$$
where $\pi_0(P)^{\rm lib}$ is the maximal free quotient of $\pi_0(P)$ and
where $P^{\rm tf}$ is the maximal finite type subgroup of $P$. Since
$\pi_0(P)^{\rm lib}$ is a free abelian group, there exists a lifting
$$\gamma:\pi_0(P)^{\rm lib} \rightarrow P$$
which is not necessarily $\sigma$-equivariant. Since $P^{\rm tf}$
is a $k$-group of finite type, the restriction of $\gamma$ to
$\Lambda=N\pi_0(P)^{\rm lib}$ for $N$ sufficiently divisible is
$\sigma$-equivariant. This gives a discrete torsion-free subgroup
$P$. Hypothesis $(3)$ is equivalent to the quotient of $M$
by this discrete torsion-free subgroup being a $k$-scheme of finite type.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Consider the quotient $X=[M/ P]$. The groupoid $X(k)$ of fixed points
under $\sigma$ has as objects pairs $x=(m,p)$ with $m\in M$ and $p\in P$
such that $p\sigma(m)=m$. A morphism $h:(m,p)\rightarrow (m',p')$ is an
element $h\in P$ such that $hm=m'$ and $p'=hp\sigma(h)^{-1}$. Let
$P_\sigma$ be the group of $\sigma$-conjugacy classes in $P$. The
$\sigma$-conjugacy class of $p$ defines an element $\cl(x)\in
P_\sigma$ which depends only on the isomorphism class of $x$. Since
$m$ is defined over a finite extension of $k$, $\cl(x)$ belongs to the
subgroup
$$\cl(x)\in \rmH^1(k,P)$$
which is the torsion part of $P_\sigma$.
\end{numero}

\begin{lemme}\label{tilde kappa}
Any character $\kappa:\rmH^1(k,P)\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ can
be extended to a character of finite order $\tilde \kappa:P_\sigma
\rightarrow \Ql^\times$.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
Let $P^0$ be the group of neutral components of $P$. According to Lang's
theorem, every element of $P^0$ is $\sigma$-conjugate to the
identity. It follows that the map $P_\sigma \rightarrow \pi_0(P)_\sigma$
from $P_\sigma$ to the group of $\sigma$-conjugacy classes of
$\pi_0(P)$ is an isomorphism. The characters $\tilde\kappa: P_\sigma
\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ are therefore the $\Ql$-points of the $\Ql$-group
diagonalizable of finite type
$(\pi_0(P)_\sigma)^*=\Spec(\Ql[\pi_0(P)_\sigma])$. The characters
$\kappa:\rmH^1(k,P)\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ form the group
$\pi_0((\pi_0(P)_\sigma)^*)$ of connected components of
$(\pi_0(P)_\sigma)^*$. Any element $\kappa\in
\pi_0((\pi_0(P)_\sigma)^*)$ can be lifted to a torsion element
$\tilde \kappa\in (\pi_0(P)_\sigma)^*$.
\end{proof}

The quotient $X=[M/ P]$ is clearly equivalent to $[(M/ \Lambda)/ (P/
\Lambda)]$ where $M/ \Lambda$ and $P/ \Lambda$ are of finite type. In
particular, the set of isomorphism classes of objects of $X(k)$
is finite and the group of automorphisms of each object is finite. For any
character $\kappa:\rmH^1(k,P)\rightarrow \Ql^\times$, we can therefore
form the finite sum
$${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=\sum_{x\in X(k)/\sim} \frac{\langle \cl(x), \kappa \rangle}
{{\sharp\,} \Aut(x)}.
$$
Let $\tilde\kappa:P \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ be a $\sigma$-invariant
character of finite order that represents $\kappa$. We can then
choose a discrete subgroup $\Lambda\subset P$ such that condition
$(3)$ of \ref{hypothese : finitude} is satisfied and such that the restriction
$\kappa$ to $\Lambda$ is trivial. We denote by $\tilde\kappa$ the
character of $P/ \Lambda$ which is thus deduced. Let $\rmH^n_c(M/
\Lambda)_{\tilde\kappa}$ be the eigenspace of $\rmH^n_c(M/
\Lambda)$ with eigenvalue $\tilde\kappa$. The following statement is an
immediate corollary of \ref{formule des points fixes}.


\begin{proposition}\label{formule des points fixes 2}
We have the formula
$$ {\sharp\,} P^0(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_{\kappa} =\sum_n (-1)^n \tr(\sigma, \rmH^n_c(M/
\Lambda)_{\tilde\kappa}).
$$
Furthermore, if $\Lambda'\subset \Lambda$ is a subgroup of maximal rank
and $\sigma$-invariant then we have a canonical isomorphism
$$\rmH^n_c(M/\Lambda')_{\tilde\kappa} \rightarrow \rmH^n_c(M/\Lambda)_{\tilde\kappa}$$
for each integer $n$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
% [journal 8.1.13] the journal DEDUCES this ("since Lambda is torsion-free, its intersection
% with P^0 is trivial"); stating it as a further hypothesis on a new Lambda detached the
% proof from the Lambda fixed earlier.
Since $\Lambda$ is a torsion-free subgroup of $P$, we have that
the intersection of $\Lambda$ with the neutral component $P^0$ is trivial.
Consequently, the homomorphism $P^0 \rightarrow P/ \Lambda$ induces an
isomorphism of $P^0$ onto the neutral component of $P/ \Lambda$. By
comparing with \ref{formule des points fixes}, we obtain the desired formula.
\end{proof}

In practice, this proposition is useful for comparing and controlling
the variation of the sum ${\sharp\,} X(k')_\kappa$ when we take points
with values in a variable finite extension $k'$ of $k$. Let $m=\deg(k'/
k)$. For any isomorphism class $x'$ of $X(k')$, we have a $\sigma^m$-conjugacy class in $P$. Since $\kappa:P\rightarrow
\Ql^\times$ is $\sigma$-invariant, it is a fortiori
$\sigma^m$-invariant, so that we can define the pairing $\langle
\cl(x),\kappa \rangle \in \Ql^\times$. We then have the formula
$$ {\sharp\,} P^0(k') {\sharp\,} X(k')_\kappa =\sum_n (-1)^n \tr(\sigma^m, \rmH^n_c(M/
\Lambda)_{\tilde\kappa}).
$$


\begin{corollaire}\label{k'->k}
Let $X=[M/ P]$ and $X'=[M'/ P']$ be as above. Let $\kappa:P
\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ and $\kappa':P' \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ be two
$\sigma$-invariant characters of finite order. Suppose there exists an
integer $m$ such that for any extension $k'/ k$ of degree $m'\geq m$, we have
$${\sharp\,} P^0(k') {\sharp\,} X(k')_\kappa= {\sharp\,} {P'}^0(k') {\sharp\,}
X'(k')_{\kappa'}.$$ Then, we have the equality
$${\sharp\,} P^0(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa= {\sharp\,} {P'}^0(k) {\sharp\,} X'(k)_{\kappa'}.$$
\end{corollaire}

We will now study two twin examples that are among the
simplest affine Springer fibers. In these cases, we can directly
calculate the numbers ${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa$.

\begin{exemple}\label{exemple : chaine infinie}
Let $A$ be a one-dimensional split torus over $k$ and $\bbX_*(A)$ its
group of cocharacters. Consider an extension
$$1\rightarrow A\rightarrow P \rightarrow \bbX_*(A) \rightarrow 1.$$
The sheaf $\underline \RHom(\bbX_*(A), A)$ is concentrated in degree $0$
with
$$\Hom(\bbX_*(A),A)=\GG_m.$$
Since $\rmH^1(k,\GG_m)=0$, we can split the exact sequence above and
in particular, there exists an isomorphism
$$P\simeq A\times \bbX_*(A).$$
In both cases, we have an isomorphism $P=\GG_m\times \ZZ$ over $\bar k$.

Consider the infinite chain of $\bbP^1$ obtained from the
disjoint union $\bigsqcup_i \bbP^n_i$ where $\bbP^n_i$ denotes the
$i$-th copy of $\bbP^1$, by gluing the infinite point $\infty_i$ of
$\bbP^1_i$ with the zero point $0_{i+1}$ of $\bbP^1_{i+1}$. The group
$\GG_m\times \ZZ$ acts on $\bigsqcup_i \bbP^n_i$ in a compatible way
with the gluing so that it still acts on the chain.

Consider a $k$-scheme $M$ equipped with an action of $P$ such that after
base change to $\bar k$, $M$ is isomorphic to the infinite chain of $\bbP^1$
equipped with the action of $\GG_m\times \ZZ$ as above. We have in particular
% [journal 8.3] the strata indices were swapped: the journal has M_0 as the P-torsor and
% M_1 as the X_*(A)-torsor. The English kept the French's Aut(x_1)=A / Aut(x_0) trivial,
% so the swapped version contradicted itself. Indices restored here and below.
a stratification $M=M_0\sqcup M_1$ where $M_0$ is a principal homogeneous space under $P$
and where $M_1$ is a principal homogeneous space under the discrete group $\bbX_*(A)$. The
groupoid $X=[M/ P]$ has essentially two objects $x_1$ and $x_0$ with
$\Aut(x_1)=A$ and $\Aut(x_0)$ trivial. We also have two cohomology classes
$$\cl_1,\cl_0 \in \rmH^1(k,P)=\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A)).$$
There are exactly three possibilities for these classes.

\begin{enumerate}
 \item If $A=\GG_m$ then $\bbX_*(A)=\ZZ$. In this case, we have
   the vanishing of $\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))$. We then have the formula
   $${\sharp\,} X(k)=1+\frac{1}{q-1}=\frac{q}{q-1}
   $$
   which is more agreeable to remember in the form
   $${\sharp\,} A(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)=q.$$
 \item If $A$ is the one-dimensional non-split torus over $k$ thus having
   $q+1$ points with values in $k$. In this case $\bbX_*(A)$ is the group $\ZZ$
   equipped with the action of $\sigma$ given by $m\mapsto -m$. In this
   case, the group $\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))$ has two elements and its
   elements can be described explicitly as follows. An
   $\bbX_*(A)$-torsor $E$ is a principal homogeneous space $E$
   under $\ZZ$ equipped with a map $\sigma:E\rightarrow E$
   compatible with the action of $\sigma$ on $\ZZ$ given above.
   For any $e\in E$, the difference $\sigma(e)-e$ is an integer whose
   parity is independent of the choice of $e$. If $\sigma(e)-e$ is even,
   $\cl(E)$ is the trivial element of $\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))$. If
   $\sigma(e)-e$ is odd, $\cl(E)$ is the non-trivial element of
   $\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))$.

   Suppose now that $\cl_0=0$. Then, there exists exactly
   one copy $\bbP^1_i$ in the infinite chain stable under $\sigma$.
   Since $A$ is the non-split torus, $\sigma$ necessarily exchanges
   $0_i$ and $\infty_i$. It follows that $\cl_1\not=0$.

   If $\kappa:\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ is the
   non-trivial character, we then have the formula
   $${\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=1-\frac{1}{q+1}=\frac{q}{q+1}
   $$
   which is more agreeable to remember in the form
   $${\sharp\,} A(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=q.$$

 \item Suppose always that $A$ is the non-split torus but
   consider the case where $\cl_0\not =0$. There exist then two copies
   $\bbP^1_i$ and $\bbP^1_{i+1}$ exchanged by $\sigma$ such that
   in the infinite chain $M$, the common point $\infty_i=
   0_{i+1}$ is $\sigma$-invariant. It follows that $\cl_1=0$. We have
   then the formula
   $${\sharp\,} A(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=-q$$
   where $\kappa:\rmH^1(k,\bbX_*(A))\rightarrow \Ql^\times$ is the
   non-trivial character.
\end{enumerate}

Assuming that $M_0$ has at least one $k$-point, we have $\cl_0=0$ so that the
third possibility is excluded. With this hypothesis, we observe
that the formula
$${\sharp\,} A(k) {\sharp\,} X(k)_\kappa=q$$
is then valid.

\end{exemple}

\subsection{Counting in an affine Springer fiber}\label{subsection : comptage Springer}

In this paragraph, we study point counting in an affine
Springer fiber, essentially following \cite[\S 15]{GKM} but with the
language developed in the previous paragraph. As in the
previous paragraph, the notation $x\in X$ will mean $x\in X(\bar k)$.
When referring to coefficients other than $\bar k$, we will specify.

Let $v\in |X|$ be a closed point of $X$. Let $F_v$ be the completion of the
field of rational functions $F$ by the $v$-adic topology and
$\calO_v$ the ring of integers of $F_v$, $k_v$ the residue field. We denote
$X_v=\Spec(\calO_v)$ and $X_v^\bullet=\Spec(F_v)$. Let $\bar F_v$ be the
$v$-adic completion of $\bar F=F\otimes_k\bar k$. Let $\bar\calO_v$
be the ring of integers of $\bar F_v$.

\begin{numero}
Let $a\in\frakc^\heartsuit(\calO_v)$ be an $X_v$-point of $\frakc$ whose
generic fiber is semi-simple and regular. The reduced affine Springer
fiber $\calM_v^{\rm red}(a)$ is a locally finite type $k$-scheme whose
set of $\bar k$-points is
$$\calM_v^{\rm red}(a)=\{g\in G(\bar F_v)/G(\bar \calO_v)| \ad(g)^{-1}\gamma_0 \in
\frakg(\bar \calO_v)\}
$$
where $\gamma_0=\epsilon(a)$ is the Kostant section at point $a$.
Since nilpotents play no role in the following discussion, we will
simply write $\calM_v(a)$ instead of $\calM_v^{\rm red}(a)$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let $J_a=a^* J$ be the inverse image of the regular centralizer. Let $J'_a$ be a
smooth $X_v$-group scheme with connected fibers and equipped with a
homomorphism $J'_a\rightarrow J_a$ which induces an isomorphism on the
generic fiber. In particular, $J'_a$ can be the group scheme
$J_a^0$ of the neutral components of $J_a$, but it will be more flexible for
applications to consider general $J'_a$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Consider the locally finite type $k$-group scheme
$\calP_v^{\rm red}(J'_a)$ whose set of $\bar k$-points is
$$\calP_v^{\rm red}(J'_a)= J_a(\bar F_v)/J'_a(\bar \calO_v).$$
Again, we will simply write $\calP_v(J'_a)$ for $\calP_v^{\rm
red}(J'_a)$ because nilpotents play no role in the following discussion.
The homomorphism $J'_a\rightarrow J_a$ induces a homomorphism
$$\calP_v(J'_a)\rightarrow \calP_v(J_a)$$
which induces an action of $\calP_v(J'_a)$ on $\calM_v(a)$. According to
\ref{quotient projectif}, this action satisfies hypothesis \ref{hypothese :
finitude} so that we can consider the number of $k$-points of the quotient
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]$ as well as the variant with a
$\kappa$-weighting. According to \ref{formule des points fixes 2}, we know
that these are finite numbers that have a cohomological interpretation.
In this paragraph, we are interested in the question of expressing these
numbers in terms of stable orbital integrals and
$\kappa$-orbital integrals.
\end{numero}

Let's begin by comparing the $\kappa$s that appear in the
counting problem \ref{subsection : comptage} and those that
appear in the definition of $\kappa$-orbital integrals
\ref{subsection : integrales orbitales}.

\begin{lemme} Assume that the special fiber of $J'_a$ is connected.
There exists a canonical isomorphism
$$\rmH^1(F_v,J_a)=\rmH^1(k, \calP_v(J'_a)).$$
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
According to a theorem of Steinberg, $\rmH^1(\bar F_v, J_a)=0$. It
follows that
$$
\rmH^1(F_v,J_a)=\rmH^1(\Gal(\bar k/ k), J_a(\bar F_v)).
$$
According to a theorem of Lang, we have the vanishing
$\rmH^1(k,J'_a(\bar\calO_v))=0$ because $J'_a$ is a smooth group scheme
with connected fibers. The lemma follows.
\end{proof}



\begin{proposition}\label{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}
Assume the special fiber of $J'_a$ is connected. Let
$\kappa:\rmH^1(F_v,J_a) \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ be a character and
$\kappa:\rmH^1(k, \calP_v(J'_a)) \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ the resulting character.
The number of $k$-points with $\kappa$-weighting of
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]$ can then be expressed as follows
$${\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v).
$$
where $1_{\frakg_v}$ is the characteristic function of $\frakg(\calO_v)$ and
where ${\rm d}t_v$ is any Haar measure of $J_a(F_v)$.

\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Recall the description of the category
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k).$$
An object of this category consists of a pair $x=(m,p)$ formed by an
element $m\in \calM_v(a)$ and an element $p\in \calP_v(J'_a)$ such
that the equality $p\sigma(m)=m$ holds. A morphism from $(m,p)$ to
$(m',p')$ in this category consists of an element $h\in
\calP_v(J'_a)$ such that $m'=hm$ and $p'=hp \sigma(h)^{-1}$.

Let $\calP_v(J'_a)_\sigma$ be the group of $\sigma$-conjugacy classes of
$\calP_v(J'_a)$ and $\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J'_a))$ the subgroup of continuous
cocycles. For each object $x=(m,p)$ as above, we define $\cl(x)\in
\calP_v(J'_a)_\sigma$ as the $\sigma$-conjugacy class of $p$ which actually
belongs to the subgroup
$$\cl(x)\in \rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J'_a))=\rmH^1(F_v,J_a).$$
Let $\gamma_0$ be the image of $a$ in $\frakg$ by the Kostant section. We
have a canonical isomorphism $J_a=I_{\gamma_0}$ \cf \ref{J a} so that
$\cl(x)$ can also be seen as an element of
$\rmH^1(F_v,I_{\gamma_0})$.

\begin{lemme}
For any object $x$ of $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)$, the class $\cl(x)$
belongs to the kernel of the homomorphism
$$\rmH^1(F_v,I_{\gamma_0})\rightarrow \rmH^1(F_v,G).$$
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
Let $x=(p,m)$ be as above. Let $g\in G(\bar F_v)$ be a representative
of $m\in G(\bar F_v)/ G(\bar\calO_v)$ and $j\in I_{\gamma_0}(\bar F_v)$ a
representative of $p$. The equality $p\sigma(m)=m$ implies that
$$g^{-1} j \sigma(g) \in G(\bar\calO_v)$$
so that this element is $\sigma$-conjugate to the neutral element
in $G(\bar F_v)$. Thus $\cl(x)\in \rmH^1(F_v,I_{\gamma_0})$ has a
trivial image in $\rmH^1(F_v,G)$.
\end{proof}

Continue the proof of \ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}. For any
element $\xi$ in the kernel of $\rmH^1(F_v,I_{\gamma_0})\rightarrow
\rmH^1(F_v,G)$, consider the subcategory
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]_\xi(k)$$
of objects in $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)$ such that $\cl(x)=\xi$. We
propose to write the number of isomorphism classes of
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]_\xi(k)$ as an orbital integral.

Fix $j_\xi \in I_{\gamma_0}(\bar F_v)$ in the $\sigma$-conjugacy class $\xi$. Let
$(m,p)$ be an object of $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]_\xi(k)$. Since $p$ is
$\sigma$-conjugate to $j_\xi$ in $\calP_v(J'_a)$, there exists $h\in
\calP_v(J'_a)$ such that $j_\xi=h^{-1}p \sigma(h)$. Then $(m,p)$ is
isomorphic to $(h^{-1}m,j_\xi)$. Consequently, the category
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]_\xi(k)$ is equivalent to the full subcategory
formed by objects of the form $(m,j_\xi)$. A morphism $(m,j_\xi)
\rightarrow (m',j_\xi)$ is an element $h\in \calP_v(J'_a)(k)$ such that
$hm=m'$. Since $J'_a$ has connected fibers, we have
$$\calP_v(J'_a)(k)=J_a(F_v)/J'_a(\calO_v).$$

Let $(m,j_\xi)$ be as above. Choose a representative $g\in G(\bar
F_v)$ of $m$. Then $g^{-1} j_\xi \sigma(g) \in G(\bar\calO_v)$. As
all elements of $G(\bar\calO_v)$ are $\sigma$-conjugate to the
neutral element, we can choose $g$ with $m=g G(\bar\calO_v)$ such that
$$g^{-1} j_\xi \sigma(g)=1.$$
Let $g,g' \in G(\bar F_v)$ be two elements satisfying the above
equation and representing the same class $m\in G(\bar F_v)/
G(\bar\calO_v)$. Then $g'=g k$ with $k\in G(\calO_v)$ so that the image
of $g$ in $G(\bar F_v)/ G(\calO_v)$ is well-determined by
$(m,j_\xi)$.

Thus the category $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)]_\xi(k)$ is equivalent to
the category $O_\xi$ whose objects are the elements $g\in G(\bar F_v)/
G(\calO_v)$ satisfying two equations
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $g^{-1} j_\xi \sigma(g)=1$
  \item $\ad(g)^{-1} \gamma_0 \in \frakg(\bar\calO_v)$
\end{enumerate}
and whose morphisms $g \rightarrow g_1$ are the elements $h\in
J_a(F_v)/J'_a(\calO_v)$ such that $g=hg_1$. Here, to make $h$ act on the
left, we used the canonical isomorphism $J_a=I_{\gamma_0}$.

Since the image of $\xi$ in $\rmH^1(F_v,G)$ is trivial, there exists
$g_\xi\in G(\bar F_v)$ such that $g_\xi^{-1}j_\xi \sigma(g_\xi)=1$. Fix such a
$G_\xi$ and set
$$\gamma_\xi=\ad(g_\xi)^{-1} \gamma_0.$$
The equation $g_\xi^{-1}j_\xi \sigma(g_\xi)=1$ implies that $\gamma_\xi \in
G(F_v)$. The $G(F_v)$-conjugacy class of $\gamma_\xi$ does not depend
on the choices of $j_\xi$ and $g_\xi$ but only on the class $\xi\in
\rmH^1(F_v,I_{\gamma_0})$.

By setting $g'=g_\xi^{-1} g$, the category $O_\xi$ can be described
as follows. Its objects are the elements $g'\in G(F_v)/ G(\calO_v)$
satisfying
$$\ad(g')^{-1}(\gamma_\xi)\in \frakg(\calO_v).$$
Its morphisms $g'\rightarrow g'_1$ are the elements $h\in
J_a(F_v)/J'_a(\calO_v)$ such that $g'=hg'_1$. Here, to make $h$ act on the
left, we used the canonical isomorphism $J_a=I_{\gamma_\xi}$ where
$I_{\gamma_\xi}$ is the centralizer of $\gamma_\xi$.

The set of isomorphism classes of $O_\xi$ is therefore the set of
double-classes
$$g'\in I_{\gamma_\xi}(F_v)\backslash G(F_v)/G(\calO_v)$$
such that $\ad(g')^{-1} \gamma_\xi\in\frakg(\calO_v)$. The automorphism group
of $g'$ is the group
$$(I_{\gamma_\xi}(F_v)\cap g'G(\calO_v){g'}^{-1}) / J'_a(\calO_v)$$
whose cardinality can be expressed in terms of volumes as follows
$$
\frac{{\rm vol}(I_{\gamma_\xi}(F_v)\cap g'G(\calO_v){g'}^{-1},{\rm d}t_v)}
{{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.
$$
We thus have
$${\sharp\,} O_\xi= \sum \frac{{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)} {{\rm
vol}(J_a(F_v)\cap g'G(\calO_v){g'}^{-1},{\rm d}t_v) }$$ the summation being
extended over the set of double classes
$$g'\in I_{\gamma_\xi}(F_v)\backslash G(F_v)/G(\calO_v)$$
such that $\ad(g')^{-1} \gamma_\xi\in\frakg(\calO_v)$. We thus obtain the
formula
$${\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)]_\xi(k)= {\sharp\,} O_\xi=
{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t){\bf O}_{\gamma_\xi}(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm
d}t_v).
$$
By summing over the kernel of $\rmH^1(F,I_{\gamma_0}) \rightarrow
\rmH^1(F,G)$, we obtain the formula
$${\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v)
$$
which was desired.
\end{proof}

We will eventually want the same type of statement for group schemes
$J'_a$ not necessarily having a connected special fiber
and in particular for $J_a$ itself. The direct counting of the
number of $k$-points of the quotient $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)]$ proves
to be very tedious. It is more economical to go through the
comparison with the connected case. We will state the result
only in the case of $J_a$, although it is generally valid.

Let $\kappa:\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a))\rightarrow \Ql^\times$. By using
the homomorphism
$$\rmH^1(k,P_v(J_a^0))\rightarrow
\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a)),
$$
we obtain a character of $\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a^0))$ and consequently a
character of $\rmH^1(F_v,J_a)$ which we will all denote by $\kappa$.

\begin{proposition}\label{comptage avec P_v(J_a)}
Consider $\kappa$ a character of $\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a))$ and
let $\kappa:\rmH^1(F_v,J_a) \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ be the character of
$\rmH^1(F_v,J_a)$ that results from it. Then, the number of $k$-points with
the $\kappa$-weighting of $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)]$ can be expressed
as follows
$$
{\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa={\rm vol}(J_a^0(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v).
$$
where $1_{\frakg_v}$ is the characteristic function of $\frakg(\calO_v)$ and
where ${\rm d}t_v$ is any Haar measure of $J_a(F_v)$.

Moreover, if $\kappa:\rmH^1(F_v,J_a) \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ is a
character that does not come from a character of
$\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a))$, then the $\kappa$-orbital integral ${\bf
O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v)$ is zero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is a comparison between $\calP_v(J_a)$ and the known case
$\calP_v(J_a^0)$. It suffices to prove the equality
$$
{\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa= {\sharp\,}
[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_\kappa
$$
in the first case and the vanishing of
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_\kappa$ in the second case. The
proof will be based on the same principle as \ref{exemple :
classifiant fini} although the details are more complicated in the present
case.


We have an exact sequence
$$
1 \rightarrow \pi_0(J_{a,v}) \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a^0) \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a) \rightarrow 1
$$
where $\pi_0(J_{a,v})$ is the group of connected components of the fiber
of $J_a$ at $v$. This implies a long exact sequence
\begin{eqnarray}
1 \rightarrow \pi_0(J_{a,v})^\sigma \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a^0)^\sigma \rightarrow
\calP_v(J_a)^\sigma \rightarrow \\
\pi_0(J_{a,v})_\sigma \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a^0)_\sigma \rightarrow
\calP_v(J_a)_\sigma \rightarrow 1
\end{eqnarray}
where the exponent $\sigma$ denotes a group of $\sigma$-invariants and
the index $\sigma$ denotes a group of $\sigma$-coinvariants.

Consider the functor
$$\pi:[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k) \rightarrow  [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k).$$
It maps an object $x'=(m,p')$ with $m\in\calM_v(a)$ and $p'\in
\calP_v(J_a^0)$ such that $p' \sigma(m)=m$ to the object $x=(m,p)$ where $p$
is the image of $p'$ in $\calP_v(J_a)$. Choose a set of
representatives $\{x_\psi\mid \psi\in \Psi\}$ of the isomorphism classes of
$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)$.

Since the homomorphism $\calP_v(J_a^0)\rightarrow \calP_v(J_a)$ is
surjective, this subcategory of $[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$ is
equivalent to $[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$ so that to
calculate the numbers ${\sharp\,} [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_\kappa$,
it is permissible to restrict to this subcategory.

For each $\psi\in \Psi$, consider the full subcategory
$$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x_\psi}$$
of $[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$ formed by objects of the form
$x'_\psi=(m_\psi,p'_\psi)$ with $p'_\psi\mapsto p_\psi$. If $\psi\not=\psi'$,
two objects $x'_\psi\in [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x_\psi}$ and
$x'_{\psi'}\in [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x_{\psi'}}$ are not
isomorphic. Moreover, for any $x'\in [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$ there
exists $\psi\in \Psi$ and $x'_{\psi}\in [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)]
(k)_{x_\psi}$ such that $x'$ and $x'_\psi$ are isomorphic. Thus, the
disjoint union of the categories
$$\bigsqcup_\psi [\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x_\psi}$$
forms a full subcategory of $[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$
which is equivalent to it. Consequently, to count the objects of
$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)$, we can count in each
$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x_\psi}$ and then sum over the
$\psi\in \Psi$.

Fix an $x_\psi$ and simply denote it as $x=(m,p)$. The category
$$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_{x}$$
is not difficult to describe. By fixing an object $x_1=(m,p_1)$ with $p_1
\mapsto p$, we identify the set of objects of this category with the
group $\pi_0(J_{a,v})$ which is the kernel of $\calP_v(J_a^0)\rightarrow
\calP_v(J_a)$. A morphism in this category is given by an
element of the group
$$H_1=\{h_1\in  \calP_v(J_a^0) \mid hm=m \ {\rm and}\ h_1 \sigma(h_1)^{-1} \in \pi_0(J_{a,v})\}.$$
The category $[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)]_x$ is equivalent to the
quotient category of the set $\pi_0(J_{a,v})$ by the action of $H_1$
with $H_1$ acting via the homomorphism $\alpha:H_1\rightarrow
\pi_0(J_{a,v})$ defined by $\alpha(h_1)=h_1 \sigma(h_1)^{-1}$. The set
of isomorphism classes of this category is identified with the cokernel
${\rm cok}(\alpha)$ of $\alpha$ and the automorphism group of each
object is identified with the kernel ${\rm ker}(\alpha)$. This shows in
particular that $H_1$ is a finite group.

Let $\kappa$ be a character of $\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a^0))$. This group
is canonically identified with the torsion part of $\calP_v(J_a^0)_\sigma$.
Consider the restriction of $\kappa$ to $\pi_0(J_{a,v})_\sigma$ and
also denote $\kappa$ as the character $\pi_0(J_{a,v})\rightarrow \Ql^\times$
that results from it. This character is certainly trivial on the image of
$\alpha:H_1 \rightarrow \pi_0(J_{a,v})_\sigma$ so that it defines a
character on the cokernel $\kappa: {\rm cok}(\alpha)\rightarrow
\Ql^\times$. The sum
$$\sum_{x'} \frac{\langle \cl(x'),\kappa \rangle}
{{\sharp\,} \Aut(x')}=0
$$
over the set of isomorphism classes of the full subcategory
$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)]_x$ is then equal to
$$
\sum_{z\in {\rm cok}(\alpha)}\frac{\langle z,\kappa \rangle}{{\sharp\,} {\rm
ker}(\alpha)}\langle \cl(x_1),\kappa \rangle.
$$
If the restriction of $\kappa$ to $\pi_0(J_{a,v})$ is non-trivial then this
sum is zero, which implies the vanishing of
$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_\kappa=0$.

Now assume that the restriction of $\kappa$ to $\pi_0(J_{a,v})$
is trivial. In this case, $\kappa$ factors through $\rmH^1(k,\calP_v(J_a))$
and the sum above is equal to
$$
\frac{{\sharp\,} {\rm cok}(\alpha)}{{\sharp\,} {\rm ker}(\alpha)} \langle \cl(x),\kappa
\rangle.
$$
It remains to calculate ${{\sharp\,} {\rm cok}(\alpha) / {\sharp\,} {\rm
ker}(\alpha)}$. The exact sequence
$$
1\rightarrow {\rm ker}(\alpha) \rightarrow H_1 \rightarrow
\pi_0(J_{a,v}) \rightarrow {\rm coker}(\alpha) \rightarrow  1
$$
implies the equality
$$\frac{{\sharp\,} {\rm cok}(\alpha)}{{\sharp\,} {\rm ker}(\alpha)} =
\frac{{\sharp\,}\pi_0(J_{a,v})}{{\sharp\,} H_1}.$$

Let $h_1\in H_1$ and $h$ its image in $\calP_v(J_a)$. Then $h
\sigma(h)^{-1}=1$ so that $h\in \calP_v(J_a)^\sigma$. Let ${\rm
stab}(m)$ be the subgroup of elements of $\calP_v(J_a)$ that stabilize
$m$. We then have the exact sequence
$$1\rightarrow \pi_0(J_{a,v})\rightarrow H_1 \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a)^\sigma\cap {\rm
stab}(m)\rightarrow 1
$$
where $\calP_v(J_a)^\sigma\cap {\rm stab}(m)$ is exactly the group of
automorphisms of ${\rm Aur}(x)$ in the category
$[\calM_v(J_a)/\calP_v(J_a)]$. We thus have the equality
$$\frac{{\sharp\,}\pi_0(J_{a,v})}{{\sharp\,} H_1}=\frac{1}{{\sharp\,}\Aut(x)}$$
which implies the equality
$${\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa= {\sharp\,} [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a^0)](k)_\kappa$$
which was desired.
\end{proof}

By applying the general results of \ref{subsection : comptage} to
the present situation, we obtain a cohomological interpretation of
stable orbital integrals and $\kappa$-orbital integrals. Take
a torsion-free $\sigma$-stable subgroup $\Lambda$ of $\calP_v(J_a^0)$
as in \ref{hypothese : finitude}. This subgroup exists by virtue of
the discussions following \ref{hypothese : finitude} and the result of
Kazhdan-Lusztig \cf \ref{quotient projectif}. We obtain the following corollary
of \ref{formule des points fixes 2} and \ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}.

\begin{corollaire}
Let $\kappa$ be a character of $\rmH^1(F_v,J_a)$. Let $J_a^{\flat,0}$ be the
neutral component of the N\'eron model of $J_a$. For any $\Lambda$
as above, we have the equality
$$
\sum_n (-1)^n \tr(\sigma, \rmH^n([\calM_v(a)/\Lambda])_\kappa)
={\rm vol}(J_a^{\flat,0}(\calO_v) ,{\rm d}t_v) {\bf O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v).
$$
\end{corollaire}

\begin{proof}
By combining \ref{formule des points fixes 2} and \ref{comptage
avec P_v(J'_a)} we obtain the formula
\begin{eqnarray*}
&\sum_n (-1)^n \tr(\sigma, \rmH^n([\calM_v(a)/\Lambda])_\kappa)\\
=&({\sharp\,}\calP_v^0(J_a^0)(k)) {\rm vol}(J_a^0(\calO_v) ,{\rm d}t_v) {\bf
O}^\kappa_a(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v).
\end{eqnarray*}
where ${\sharp\,} \calP_v^0(J_a^0)(k)$ is the number of $k$-points of the
neutral component of $\calP_v(J_a^0)$. If $J_a^{\flat,0}$ is the
neutral component of the N\'eron model, we have a homomorphism
$J_a^0 \rightarrow J_a^{\flat,0}$ which induces an exact sequence
$$1 \rightarrow J_a^{\flat,0}(\bar\calO_v)/J_a^0(\bar\calO_v) \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a^0)
\rightarrow \calP_v(J_a^{\flat,0}) \rightarrow 1
$$
which allows identifying the neutral component $\calP_v^0(J_a^0)$ of
$\calP_v(J_a^0)$ with the connected affine $k$-group whose $\bar
k$-points are $ J_a^{\flat,0}(\bar\calO_v)/J_a^0(\bar\calO_v)$. Since
$J_a^0$ and $J_a^{\flat,0}$ are group schemes with connected
fibers, we deduce an exact sequence
$$1 \rightarrow J_a^0(\calO_v) \rightarrow J_a^{\flat,0}(\calO_v) \rightarrow
\calP_v^0(J_a^0)(k) \rightarrow 1$$ from which the equality
$$({\sharp\,} \calP_v^0(J_a^0)(k))
{\rm vol}(J_a^0(\calO_v) ,{\rm d}t_v) = {\rm vol}(J_a^{\flat,0}(\calO_v)
,{\rm d}t_v) $$ for any Haar measure ${\rm d}t_v$ on
$J(F_v)$. The corollary follows.
\end{proof}


\subsection{A very simple case}
\label{subsection : fibre simple}

Let $v$ be a closed point of $X$. Let $k_v$ be the residue field of $v$.
Choose a geometric point $\bar v$ above $v$. Let
$X_v=\Spec(\calO_v)$ be the completion of $X$ at $v$ and
$X_v^\bullet=\Spec(F_v)$ where $F_v$ is the fraction field of
$\calO_v$. Choose a uniformizer $\epsilon_v$. In this paragraph,
we denote $G$ as the restriction of $G$ to $X_v$ and $G^\bullet$ as the
restriction of $G$ to $X_v^\bullet$.


\begin{numero}
Let $a\in \frakc(\calO_v)$ whose image in $\frakc(F_v)$ is regular
and semi-simple. Let $d_v(a)={\rm deg}_v (a^* \discrim_G)\in \NN$. We
will assume that
$$d_v(a) =2\ {\rm and}\ c_v(a)=0.$$
According to Bezrukavnikov's formula \cf \ref{dimension Bezru}, the
dimension of the affine Springer fiber $\calM_v(a)$ is then equal to
one. We will show that over $\bar k$, this affine Springer fiber
is a disjoint union of copies of the infinite chain of projective
lines.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let $\gamma_0=\epsilon(a)\in \frakg(F_v)$ be the Kostant section
applied to $a$. Its centralizer $T^\bullet=I_{\gamma_0}$ is a maximal
torus of $G^\bullet$. The hypothesis $c_v(a)=0$ implies that
$T^\bullet$ is an unramified maximal torus, meaning it extends to a
maximal torus $T$ of $G$. Let $\Phi$ be the set of roots of $\bar
T=T\otimes_{\calO_v} {\bar\calO_{\bar v}}$. We then have the root
valuation function of Goresky, Kottwitz and MacPherson \cf
\cite{GKM-codim}
$$r_a:\Phi\rightarrow \NN$$
defined by $r_a(\alpha)={\rm val}(\alpha(\gamma_0))$ where we have taken the
valuation on $\bar F_{\bar v}$ which extends the valuation on $F_v$. We then have
$$d_v(a)=\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi} r_\alpha(\gamma_0).$$
Thus, there exists a unique pair of roots $\pm \alpha$ such that $r_{\pm
\alpha}(\gamma_0)=1$ and $r_{\alpha'}(\gamma_0)=0$ for any root
$\alpha'\notin \{\pm \alpha\}$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
The Galois group $\Gal(\bar k/ k_v)$ acts on $\Phi$ by leaving the
function $r_a(\alpha)$ invariant, so that it stabilizes the pair $\{\pm
\alpha\}$. Let $\bar G_{\pm \alpha}$ be the subgroup of $\bar
G=G\otimes_{\calO_v}{\bar \calO_{\bar v}}$ generated by $\bar T$ and by
the root subgroups $U_\alpha$ and $U_{-\alpha}$. The action of the
Galois group $\Gal(\bar k/ k_v)$ on $\bar T$ stabilizing $\{\pm\alpha\}$
allows descending $\bar G_{\pm \alpha}$ to a reductive group sub-scheme
$G_{\pm \alpha}$ of $G_{\calO_v}$. The center $Z_{\pm \alpha}$
of $G_{\pm \alpha}$ which is identified with the kernel of
$\alpha:T\rightarrow\GG_m$ is also defined over $\calO_v$. Let $A_{\pm
\alpha}=T/Z_{\pm \alpha}$; this is a one-dimensional torus over $X_v$.
\end{numero}

\begin{proposition}
We have a canonical homomorphism $J_a\rightarrow T$ whose image at the
level of $\bar\calO_{\bar v}$-points is the kernel of the composite
homomorphism of the reduction modulo the maximal ideal $T(\bar\calO_{\bar
v})\rightarrow T(\bar k)$ and the root $\alpha:T(\bar k)\rightarrow
\GG_m(\bar k)$.
\end{proposition}

We deduce the following description of $\calP_v(J_a)$.

\begin{corollaire}
We have an exact sequence of abelian groups with the action of the
Frobenius endomorphism $\sigma$
$$1 \rightarrow A_{\pm \alpha}(\bar k)\rightarrow \calP_{\bar v}(J_a)(\bar k) \rightarrow
\bbX_*(T)\rightarrow 1
$$
where $\bbX_*(T)$ is the group of cocharacters of the torus $T$ above
$\bar \calO_{\bar v}$.
\end{corollaire}

\begin{proof}
From the exact sequence
$$1\rightarrow J_a(\bar\calO_{\bar v}) \rightarrow T(\bar\calO_{\bar v})\rightarrow
A_{\pm \alpha}(\bar k)\rightarrow 1
$$
we deduce the exact sequence
$$1 \rightarrow A_{\pm \alpha}(\bar k) \rightarrow J_a(\bar F_{\bar v})/J_a(\bar\calO_{\bar v})
\rightarrow T(\bar F_v)/T(\bar \calO_v) \rightarrow 1.
$$
We also have an isomorphism
$$T(\bar F_v)/T(\bar \calO_v)=\bbX_*(T)$$
which is deduced from the homomorphism $\bbX_*(T) \rightarrow T(\bar F_v)$
defined by $\lambda\mapsto \epsilon_v^\lambda$, whence the corollary.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemme}
The $\bar k$-points of the affine Springer fiber
$$\calM_{\bar v}(a)(\bar k)=\{g\in G(\bar F_{\bar v})/ G(\bar\calO_{\bar v})
| \ad(g)^{-1}(\gamma_0)\in \frakg(\bar\calO_{\bar v})\}
$$
can be uniquely written in the form
$$g=\epsilon_v^\lambda U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1}) $$
with $\lambda\in \bbX_*(T)$ and $x\in \bar k$.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
With the Iwasawa decomposition, for any element $g\in G(\bar
F_{\bar v})/ G(\bar\calO_{\bar v})$, there exist unique $\lambda\in \bbX_*(T)$
and $u\in U(\bar F_{\bar v})/ U(\bar\calO_{\bar v})$ such that
$$g=\epsilon_v^\lambda u .$$
As $T_{\bar v}$ commutes with $\gamma_0$, the embedding
$$\calM_{\bar v}(a) \rightarrow \calG_{\bar v}=G(\bar F_{\bar
v})/ G(\bar\calO_{\bar v})
$$
is $T_{\bar v}$-equivariant. The action of $T_{\bar v}$ on $\calM_v(a)$
factors through
$$T_{\bar v} \rightarrow T(\bar F_{\bar v}) \rightarrow \calP_v(J_a)
=T(\bar F_{\bar v})/ J_a(\bar \calO_{\bar v})$$
so that it factors through the one-dimensional torus
$$T_{\bar v}\rightarrow A_{\pm \alpha, \bar v}.$$
It follows that if we write $g\in \calM_v(a)(\bar k)$ in the form
$g=\epsilon_v^\lambda u $, $u$ must be of the form $u=U_\alpha(y)$
with $y\in \bar F_{\bar v}/ \bar \calO_{\bar v}$ uniquely determined.
A calculation in $\SL_2$ \cf \cite[lemma 6.2]{GKM} then shows that with
the hypothesis $r_{\pm \alpha}(a)=1$, $y$ is uniquely written in the form
$y=x \epsilon_v^{-1}$ with $x\in \bar k$ and conversely, the elements $g$
of the form $g=\epsilon_v^\lambda U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1})$
belong to $\calM_{\bar v}(a)(\bar k)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemme}
The fixed points of the one-dimensional torus $A_{\pm \alpha,\bar v}$ in
$\calM_{\bar v}$ are $\epsilon_v^\lambda$. For fixed $\lambda\in \bbX_*(T)$,
$A_{\pm \alpha,\bar v}$ acts simply transitively on
$$O_\lambda=\{\epsilon_v^\lambda U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1}) \in \calM_v(a)(\bar k)
| x\in \bar k^\times\}.
$$
Furthermore, the boundary of the closure of this orbit consists of
$\epsilon_v^\lambda$ and $\epsilon_v^{\lambda-\alpha^\vee}$ where
$\alpha^\vee$ is the coroot associated with the root $\alpha$.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
A direct calculation shows that the $\epsilon_v^\lambda$ are fixed under
the action of $A_{\pm \alpha,\bar v}$ and that $A_{\pm \alpha,\bar v}$ acts
simply transitively on $O_\lambda$. This shows that the set
of fixed points of $A_{\pm \alpha,\bar v}$ is exactly
$\{\epsilon_v^\lambda| \lambda\in \bbX_*(T)\}$. When $x\rightarrow 0$,
$\epsilon_v^\lambda U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1})$ tends towards
$\epsilon_v^\lambda$ so that $\epsilon_v^\lambda$ belongs to
the closure of $O_\lambda$.

It remains to prove that when $x\rightarrow \infty$,
$\epsilon_v^\lambda U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1})$ tends towards
$\epsilon_v^{\lambda-\alpha^\vee}$. It is equivalent to prove
that $U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1})$ tends towards $\epsilon_v^{-\alpha^\vee}$
when $x\rightarrow \infty$. This is a well-known calculation in the
affine Grassmannian which follows from Steinberg's relation in
$G(\bar F_{\bar v})$ \cf \cite[chap. 3, lemma 19]{St}
$$y^{-\alpha^\vee} w_\alpha= U_{-\alpha}(y) U_{\alpha}(-y^{-1}) U_\alpha(y)$$
which holds for any $y\in \bar F_{\bar v}$, for any root $\alpha$ and
for a representative $w_\alpha$ of the reflection $s_\alpha\in W$
attached to the root $\alpha$ independent of $y$ and which in particular
belongs to $G(\bar k)$. By taking $y=-x^{-1} \epsilon_v$ with $x\in
\bar k^\times$, we obtain the following relation in $G(\bar F_{\bar v})/
G(\bar \calO_{\bar v})$
$$U_\alpha(x \epsilon_v^{-1})=\epsilon_v^{-\alpha^\vee} U_{-\alpha}(-x^{-1}\epsilon_v^{-1}).$$
As $x$ tends to $\infty$, we observe that $U_\alpha(x
\epsilon_v^{-1})$ tends towards $\epsilon_v^{-\alpha^\vee}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{comptage fibre simple}
Let $\kappa\in \hat T^\sigma$ be a torsion element such that
$$\kappa(\alpha^\vee)\not =1.$$
Then, we have the formula
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa=  \sharp\, A_{\pm\alpha}(k_v)^{-1} q^{\deg(v)}.$$
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The affine Springer fiber $\calM_v(a)$ is obtained as the restriction of
scalars $k_v/ k$ of an affine Springer fiber defined over $k_v$. The same
applies to $\calP_v(J_a)$ and the quotient $[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)]$.
We can therefore assume that $k_v=k$.

Let $A=A_{\pm \alpha,v}$ which is a one-dimensional torus over $k$.
Consider the $\sigma$-invariant subgroup of $\bbX_*(T)$ generated
by $\alpha^\vee$ and consider the inverse image of the exact sequence
$$1\rightarrow A\rightarrow \calP_v(J_a) \rightarrow \bbX_*(T) \rightarrow 1$$
by the homomorphism $\ZZ  \alpha^\vee \rightarrow \bbX_*(T)$. This is an
algebraic group $P$ defined over $k$ equipped with an exact sequence
$$1\rightarrow A \rightarrow P \rightarrow \ZZ  \alpha^\vee \rightarrow 1$$
and an injective homomorphism $P\rightarrow\calP_v(J_a)$ which induces
the identity on the neutral component $A$.

In the affine Springer fiber $\calM_v(a)$, we have a $k$-point $m$
given by the Kostant section. According to the description above of
$\calM_v(a)\otimes_k \bar k$, the connected component $M$ of
$\calM_v(a)\otimes_k \bar k$ is an infinite chain of projective
lines equipped with an action of $P\otimes_k \bar k$ which is simply
transitive on $M^\reg$. Since $M$ contains the $k$-point $m$, it is
defined over $k$. We recover $\calM_v(J_a)$ from $M$ by
the induction of $P$ into $\calP_v(J_a)$
$$\calM_v(a)=M \wedge^P \calP_v(J_a).$$
In particular, we have an equivalence of categories
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)]=[M/ P].$$
We are thus reduced to the point counting exercise already solved
in example \ref{exemple : chaine infinie}.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Counting in an anisotropic Hitchin fiber}
\label{subsection : comptage Hitchin}

Recall the point counting in a Hitchin fiber $\calM_a$ with
$a\in\calM_a^\ani(k)$ following paragraph 9 of \cite{N}. In {\em loc.
cit.}, we considered the quotient of $\calM_a$ by $\calP_a$. It is
in fact more convenient to consider a more
general quotient.

\begin{numero}\label{choix J'_a}
Let $J'_a$ be a smooth commutative finite type $X$-group scheme equipped
with a homomorphism $J'_a\rightarrow J_a$ which is an isomorphism on a
non-empty open set $U$ of $X$. The data of $J'_a$ is equivalent to the
data of open compact subgroups $J'_a(\calO_v)\subset
J_a(\calO_v)$ for points $v\in |X-U|$. Let $\calP'_a=\calP(J'_a)$ be the
classifying stack of $J'_a$-torsors over $X$. We then have a homomorphism
$\calP'_a\rightarrow \calP_a$ which induces an action of $\calP'_a$ on
$\calM_a$. For $J'_a$ sufficiently small in the sense that the open
compact subgroups $J'_a(\calO_v)$ are sufficiently small, $\rmH^0(\bar X,J'_a)$ is
trivial and so $\calP'_a$ is representable by a locally finite type
algebraic group over $k$. For counting, it will be convenient to assume that
the fibers of $J'_a$ are all connected.
Consider the quotient category $[\calM_a /\calP'_a]$.
\end{numero}

The counting of $k$-points of $\calM_a$ is based on the following form
of the product formula \cf \ref{produit} and \cite[theorem 4.6]{N}.

\begin{proposition}\label{produit quotient}
Let $U=a^{-1}(\frakc_D^\rs)$ be the inverse image of the regular semi-simple
locus of $\frakc_D$. We have an equivalence of categories
$$[\calM_a/ \calP'_a]=\prod_{v\in X-U} [\calM_v(a)/ \calP_v(J'_a)]$$
compatible with the action of $\sigma\in\Gal(\bar k/ k)$. In particular, we have
an equivalence between the categories of $k$-points
$$[\calM_a/ \calP'_a](k)=\prod_{v \in |X-U|} [\calM_v(a)/ \calP_v(J'_a)](k).$$
\end{proposition}

Let $(\calP'_a)^0$ be the neutral component of $\calP'_a$. Since
$a\in\calA^\ani(k)$, the group of connected components $\pi_0(\calP_a)$
is a finite group equipped with an action of the Frobenius element
$\sigma\in\Gal(\bar k/ k)$.


\begin{proposition}
Assume that the fibers of $J'_a$ are connected. For any $\sigma$-invariant
character of $\pi_0(\calP_a)$
$$\kappa:\pi_0(\calP_a)_\sigma\rightarrow \Ql^\times,$$
we have
$$\sharp\,[\calM_a/ \calP'_a](k)_\kappa=\prod_{v \in |X-U|} \sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/ \calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa
.$$
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For any closed point $v$ of $X$ in the complement of the open set
$U=a^{-a}(\frakc_D^\rs)$, the composite homomorphism
$$\calP_v(J'_a)\rightarrow \calP'_a \rightarrow \pi_0(\calP'_a)$$
factors through $\pi_0(\calP_v(J'_a))$. The homomorphism
$\kappa:\pi_0(\calP'_a)_\sigma \rightarrow \Ql^\times$ therefore defines a
homomorphism $\kappa:\pi_0(\calP_v(J'_a))_\sigma \rightarrow
\Ql^\times$.

In \ref{produit quotient}, if a point $y\in [\calM_a/ \calP'_a](k)$
corresponds to a collection of points $y_v\in [\calM_v(a)/
\calP_v(J'_a)](k)$ for $v\in |X-U|$, then we have the formula
$$\langle \cl(y),\kappa \rangle =\prod_{v\in |X-U|}  \langle \cl(y_v),\kappa \rangle.$$
Consequently, we have the factorization
$$\sharp\,[\calM_a/ \calP'_a](k)_\kappa=\prod_{v\in |X-U|}
\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa$$
whence the proposition.
\end{proof}

The conjunction with \ref{formule des points fixes} gives the following
corollary.

\begin{corollaire}\label{formule des points fixes Hitchin}
For any character $\kappa:\pi_0(\calP_a)_\sigma\rightarrow
\Ql^\times$, we have
$$\sum_n (-1)^n\tr(\sigma, \rmH^n(\calM_a)_\kappa)
= {\sharp\,}
(\calP'_a)^0(k)\prod_{v\in |X-U|}
\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa
$$
where $\rmH^n(\calM_a)_\kappa$ is the eigenspace of
$\rmH^n(\calM_a)$ where the group $\pi_0(\calP_a)$ acts via the
character $\kappa$.
\end{corollaire}

\begin{numero}\label{trivialisation de D'}
To express the numbers $\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/ \calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa$
in terms of local orbital integrals, make the following choices:
\begin{itemize}
  \item at every place $v\in |X-U|$, choose a trivialization of the
      invertible bundle $D'|_{X_v}$,
  \item at every place $v\in |X-U|$, choose a Haar measure
      ${\rm d}t_v$ of the torus $J_a(F_v)$.
\end{itemize}
These choices allow us to:
\begin{itemize}
  \item identify the restriction of $a$ to $X_v$ with an element
      $a_v\in\frakc(\calO_v)\cap \frakc^\rs(F_v)$,
  \item identify the group schemes $J_a$ and $J_{a_v}$ which
      equips $J_{a_v}(F_v)$ with a Haar measure ${\rm d}t_v$,
  \item identify the affine Springer fiber $\calM_v(a)$ equipped with
      the action of $\calP_v(J_a)$ with the affine Springer fiber
      $\calM_v(a_v)$ equipped with the action of $\calP_v(J_{a_v})$.
\end{itemize}
\end{numero}
With these choices made, we can express the numbers $\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/
\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa$ in terms of $\kappa$-orbital integrals \cf
\ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}
$$\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/ \calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf O}^\kappa_{a_v}(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm
d}t_v).
$$
We then obtain the formula
$$\sum_n (-1)^n\tr(\sigma, \rmH^n(\calM_a)_\kappa)=
(\calP'_a)^0(k) \prod_{v\in |X-U|} {\rm vol} (J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf
O}^\kappa_{a_v}(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v).$$


% [journal 8.5] heading was left untranslated ("Stabilisation sur ...").
\subsection{Stabilization over $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm ani} -\tilde\calA_H^{\rm bad}$}
\label{subsection : A good}

We are in a position to prove Theorem \ref{stabilisation sur tilde
A} on the open set $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}=\tilde\calA_H^{\rm ani}
-\tilde\calA_H^{\rm bad}$. Here $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm bad}$ is the closed sub-scheme of $\tilde\calA_H^\ani$ defined in \ref{delta bad}.

\begin{proof}
According to the support theorem \ref{support faible}, on
$\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}$, the pure perverse sheaves
$$K^n_\kappa=\tilde\nu^*\, ^p\rmH^{n}(\tilde f^\ani_*\Ql)_{\kappa}
\ {\rm and}\ K_{H,{\rm st}}^{n}=\,^p\rmH^{n+2r_H^G(D)}(\tilde
f^\ani_{H,*}\Ql)_{\rm st}(-r_H^G(D))
$$
are the intermediate extensions of their restrictions to any non-empty open set
$\tilde\calU$ of $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}$. To show that
$K^n$ and $K^n_H$ are isomorphic on $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}\otimes_k
\bar k$ and are isomorphic after semi-simplification on
$\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}$, it suffices to do so on any dense open set
$\tilde\calU$ of $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}$. We will construct a good
open set $\tilde\calU$ as follows.

Recall that we have an equality of divisors \cf \ref{discrimnant resultant}
$$ \nu^* \discrim_{G,D}= \discrim_{H,D}+2\resultant_{H,D}^G$$
on $\frakc_{H,D}$. Moreover, we know that $\discrim_{H,D}$ and
$\resultant_{H,D}^G$ are reduced divisors of $\frakc_{H,D}$
which are disjoint, so that the union
$$\discrim_{H,D}+\resultant_{H,D}^G$$
is also a reduced divisor.

\begin{lemme} Assume that $\deg(D)> 2g$. Then,
the set of $a_H\in \calA_H(\bar k)$ such that $a_H(\bar X)$ intersects
transversally $\discrim_{H,D}+\resultant_{H,D}^G$ forms a non-empty open set
$\calU$ of $\calA_H$.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
The proof is identical to the proof of \ref{A diamond
non vide}.
\end{proof}

\begin{numero}
Consider the inverse image $\tilde\calU$ of $\calU$ in
$\tilde\calA$. By shrinking this open set if necessary, we can assume
that $\tilde\calU\subset \tilde\calA^{\rm good}$. By shrinking it further if
necessary, we can assume that for all $n\in\ZZ$, the restrictions
$$L^n_\kappa=K^n_\kappa|_{\tilde\calU}\ {\rm and}\ L_{H,{\rm st}}^n=K_{H,{\rm st}}^n|_{\tilde\calU}
$$
are pure local systems of weight $n$ on $\tilde\calU$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
It suffices in fact to show that for any finite extension $k'$ of $k$,
for any $k'$-point $\tilde a_H\in \tilde\calU(k')$ with image $\tilde
a\in\tilde\calA(k')$, we have the equality of traces
\begin{equation}\label{egalite de traces}
\sum_{n} (-1)^{n}\tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_\kappa)_{\tilde a}) \\
=\sum_{n} (-1)^{n} \tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_{H,{\rm st}})_{\tilde a_H}).
\end{equation}
Indeed, we then have the equality
$$
\sum_{n} (-1)^{n}\tr(\sigma_{k'}^j,(L^n_\kappa)_{\tilde a}) \\
=\sum_{n} (-1)^{n} \tr(\sigma_{k'}^j,(L^n_{H,{\rm st}})_{\tilde a_H}).
$$
for any natural integer $j$ by considering $\tilde a_H$ as a point
with values in the extension of degree $j$ of $k'$. Moreover, as the
eigenvalues of $\sigma_{k'}$ in $(L^n_\kappa)_{\tilde a}$ and
$(L^n_{H,{\rm st}})_{\tilde a_H}$ are all of absolute value
$q^{n\deg(k'/ k)/ 2}$, these equalities imply that for any finite extension
$k'$ of $k$, for any $\tilde a_H\in \tilde\calU(k')$ with image $\tilde
a\in \tilde\calA$ and for any $n$, we have
$$\tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_\kappa)_{\tilde a})=\tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_{H,{\rm
st}})_{\tilde a_H}).$$ According to Chebotarev's theorem, this
