implies that $L^n_\kappa$ and $L^n_{H,{\rm st}}$ are isomorphic after
semi-simplification.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let's now prove formula (\ref{egalite de traces}). For any
point $\tilde a_H \in\tilde\calU(k')$, we can directly calculate both
sides of this formula and then compare them. By replacing $k$
with $k'$ and $X$ with $X\otimes_k k'$, we can assume that $\tilde a_H$ is
a $k$-point of $\tilde\calU$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let $a_H\in \calA_H(k)$ be the image of $\tilde a_H$. Let $a$ be the image of $a_H$
in $\calA(k)$. According to \ref{J JH}, we have a canonical homomorphism
$$J_a\rightarrow J_{H,a_H}$$
which is an isomorphism above the open set $U=a^{-1}(\frakc_D^\rs)$.
Choose a group scheme $J'_a$ which is smooth, commutative, with connected
fibers, and equipped with a homomorphism $J'_a\rightarrow J_a$ which is
generically an isomorphism. Let $\calP'_a$ be the classifying stack of
$J'_a$-torsors over $X$. By shrinking $J'_a$ if necessary, we can
assume that $\rmH^0(\bar X,J'_a)$ is trivial, in which case $\calP'_a$ is a
finite type algebraic group. We have natural homomorphisms
$\calP'_a \rightarrow \calP_a$ and $\calP'_a \rightarrow \calP_{H,a_H}$ which
induce an action of $\calP'_a$ on $\calM_a$ and $\calM_{H,a_H}$. We can
calculate both sides of formula \ref{egalite de traces} by
considering the quotients $[\calM_a/\calP'_a]$ and
$[\calM_{H,a_H}/\calP'_a]$. According to \ref{formule des points fixes Hitchin},
the left side of \ref{egalite de traces} is
$$(\calP'_a)^0(k) \prod_{v\in |X-U|} \sharp\,
[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa
$$
while the right side is
$$q^{r_H^G(D)}(\calP'_a)^0(k) \prod_{v\in |X-U|} \sharp\,
[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k).
$$
\end{numero}


As
$$r_H^G(D)=\sum_v \deg(v) r_{H,v}^G(a_H)$$
where $r_{H,v}^G(a_H)$ is the degree of the divisor $a_H^*
\resultant_{H,D}^G$ at $v$, it suffices to prove the following statement which
is a particularly simple case of the fundamental lemma of
Langlands-Shelstad \ref{LS}. Note that this special case of the fundamental
lemma is essentially contained in the article \cite{LL} by Labesse
and Langlands on $\SL(2)$ and was revisited from a more geometric perspective in \cite{GKM} by
Goresky, Kottwitz and MacPherson.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemme}\label{LF simple}
Let $\tilde a_H \in \tilde\calA_H(k)$. Let $v$ be a place of $X$ above
which $a_H(\bar X_v)$ does not intersect or intersects transversally the divisor
$\discrim_{H,D}+\resultant_{H,D}^G$. We have the equality between the
numbers
$$\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
q^{\deg(v)r_{H,v}^G(a_H) } \sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)
$$
which are non-zero rational numbers.
\end{lemme}

\begin{proof}
Let ${\bar v}$ be a geometric point above $v$. As $a_H(\bar
X_v)$ intersects transversally $\discrim_{H,D}+\resultant_{H,D}^G$, there are
three possibilities for the integers $d_{H,\bar v}(a_H), d_{\bar v}(a)$ and
$r_{H,v}^G(a_H)$:
\begin{enumerate}
 \item if $a_H(\bar v)\notin \discrim_{H,D}\cup \resultant_{H,D}^G$,
   then
$$d_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0, d_{\bar v}(a)=0\ {\rm and}\ r_{H,v}^G(a_H)=0,$$
 \item if $a_H(\bar v)\in \discrim_{H,D}$ then $a_H(\bar v)\notin
   \resultant_{H,D}^G$ and we have
$$d_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=1, d_{\bar v}(a)=1 \ {\rm and}\ r_{H,v}^G(a_H)=0,$$
 \item if $a_H(\bar v)\in \resultant_{H,D}^G$ then $a_H(\bar v)\notin
   \discrim_{H,D}$ and we have
$$d_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0, d_{\bar v}(a)=2\ {\rm and}\ r_{H,v}^G(a_H)=1.$$
\end{enumerate}

\begin{numero}
In the first two cases, Bezrukavnikov's formula \ref{dimension
Bezru} shows that $\delta_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=\delta_{\bar v}(a)=0$ so that
the affine Springer fibers $\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$ and $\calM_v(a)$ are
both of dimension zero. It follows that $\calP_v(J_a)$ acts
simply transitively on $\calM_v(a)$ and $\calP_v(J_{H,a_H})$ acts
simply transitively on $\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$ \cf \ref{dim zero}.
Choose a trivialization of $D'$ above $X_v$ as in
\ref{trivialisation de D'} to be able to write the orbital
integrals nicely. In particular, $a_H$ and $a$ define elements
$a_{H,v}\in \frakc_H(\calO_v)$ and $a_v\in\frakc(\calO_v)$. In conjunction
with \ref{comptage avec P_v(J_a)}, we deduce the formula
$$1=[\calM_{H,v}(a_H)/\calP_v(J_{H,a_H})](k)
={\rm vol}(J^0_{H,a_H}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf
SO}_{a_{H,v}}(1_{\frakh_v},{\rm d}t_v)
$$
Comparing with formula \ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}
$$\sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)=
{\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v){\bf SO}_{a_{H,v}}(1_{\frakh_v},{\rm
d}t_v)
$$
we obtain
$$\sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)=\frac{{\rm vol} (J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm
d}t_v)}{{\rm vol}(J^0_{H,a_H}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.$$

Noting that $\calM_v(a)$ is given with a $k$-point by the
Kostant section, we also have
$$\sharp\, [\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa=1.$$
The same reasoning as above then implies
$$\sharp\,[\calM_{v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=\frac{{\rm vol} (J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm
d}t_v)}{{\rm vol}(J^0_{a}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.
$$
It remains to note that in the first two cases, the homomorphism
$J_a\rightarrow J_{H,a_H}$ induces an isomorphism on the neutral components
$J_a^0\rightarrow J_{H,a_H}^0$ and we obtain the equality
$$\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
\sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)
$$
which was desired.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Consider now the third case where $a_H(X_v)$ intersects
transversally $\resultant_{H,D}^G$ and does not intersect $\frakc_{H,D}$.
In this case, $d_{H,\bar v}(a_H)$ is zero, so that
$$\delta_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0\ {\rm and}\ c_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0.$$
As $\delta_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0$, the affine Springer fiber
$\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$ is of dimension zero. According to \ref{dim zero},
$\calP_v(J_{H,a_H})$ acts simply transitively on
$\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$ so that we still have
$$\sharp\, [\calM_{H,v}(a_H)/\calP_v(J_{H,a_H})](k)=1.$$
The same reasoning as above shows that
$$\sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)=\frac{{\rm vol} (J'_a(\calO_v),{\rm
d}t_v) }{ {\rm vol}(J^0_{H,a_H}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.
$$
Note that since $a_H(X_v)$ does not intersect $\frakc_{H,D}$,
$J_{H,a_H}$ is a torus and in particular $J_{H,a_H}=J_{H,a_H}^0$.

Since the invariant $c_{\bar v}(a)$ depends only on the generic fiber
of $J_a|_{X_v}$, we have
$$c_{\bar v}(a)=c_{H,\bar v}(a_H)=0.$$
This implies that the affine Springer fiber $\calM_{\bar v}(a)$ is of
dimension
$$\delta_{\bar v}(a)=\frac{d_{\bar v}(a)-c_{\bar v}(a) }{ 2}=1.$$
We are therefore exactly in the situation of \ref{subsection : fibre simple}.
By applying formula \ref{comptage fibre simple} while noting that
the hypothesis $\kappa(\alpha^\vee)\not=1$ is well verified here, we
obtain the formula
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J_a)](k)_\kappa= \sharp\, A_{\pm\alpha}(k_v) q^{\deg(v)}$$
where $A_{\pm \alpha}$ is the one-dimensional torus over $k_v$ defined by
$$A_{\pm \alpha}(\bar k)=J_{H,a_H}(\calO_{\bar v})/ J_a(\calO_{\bar v}).$$
By applying formula \ref{comptage avec P_v(J_a)}, we find
$${\bf O}_{a}^\kappa(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v)= \frac{q^{\deg(v)}
}{ \sharp\, A_{\pm\alpha}(k_v) {\rm vol}(J^0_{a}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.
$$
We can also verify the formula
$$\sharp\, A_{\pm\alpha}(k_v){\rm vol}(J^0_{a}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)=
{\rm vol}(J^0_{H,a_H}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)$$ which implies
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa= \frac{ q^{\deg(v)} {\rm vol}(J'_a(\calO_v, {\rm d}t_v))
}{ {\rm vol}(J^0_{H,a_H}(\calO_v),{\rm d}t_v)}.
$$
We therefore obtain the equality
$$\sharp\,[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=
q^{\deg(v)}\sharp\,[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)
$$
which was desired.
\end{numero}

This completes the proof of \ref{LF simple}.
\end{proof}


\subsection{The fundamental lemma of Langlands-Shelstad}
\label{lemme fondamental}

We are now able to prove the fundamental lemma of
Langlands and Shelstad \ref{LS}.

\begin{proof}
Let $X_v=\Spec(\calO_v)$ with $\calO_v=k[[\epsilon_v]]$ and
$X_v^\bullet=\Spec(F_v)$ with $F_v=k((\epsilon_v))$. Let $G_v$ be a
reductive group over $X_v$, a quasi-split form of $\GG$ given by
an $\Out(\GG)$-torsor $\rho_{G,v}$ over $X_v$. Let
$(\kappa,\rho_{\kappa,v})$ be an elliptic endoscopic datum \cf
\ref{subsection : Groupes endoscopiques}. Let $H_v$ be the associated
endoscopic group. Let $a_H\in \frakc_{H}(\calO_v)$ with image $a\in
\frakc(\calO_v)\cap \frakc^\rs(F_v)$.

\begin{numero}
If the center of $G_v$ contains a split torus $C$, $C$ is also
contained in the center of $H_v$. By replacing $G_v$ with $G_v/C$ and
$H_v$ with $H_v/C$, the $\kappa$-orbital integral and the stable orbital
integral considered do not change, so we can assume that the
center of $G_v$ does not contain a split torus.

If the center of $H_v$ contains a split torus $C$, $C$ is
naturally included in the torus $I_{\gamma_0}$ where
$\gamma_0=\epsilon(a)$. The centralizer $M_v$ of $C$ in $G_v$ is a
Levi subgroup of $G_v$. By the descent formula, we can
replace the $\kappa$-orbital integrals in $G_v$ with a
$\kappa$-orbital integral in $M_v$. The center of $M_v$ now contains
a split torus, and we are reduced to the situation discussed
above. After a finite number of these reductions, we can assume that
the centers of $G_v$ and $H_v$ do not contain split tori.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let $\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$ and $\calM_v(a)$ be the associated affine Springer
fibers. According to \ref{subsection : approximation}, there exists a natural
integer $N$ such that for any finite extension $k'$ of $k$, for any $a'_H
\in \frakc_H(\calO_v\otimes_k k')$ such that
$$a_H \equiv a'_H \mod \epsilon_v^N$$
then
\begin{itemize}
 \item $a'_H$ has an image $a'\in \frakc(\calO_v\otimes_k k')\cap
   \frakc^\rs(F_v\otimes_k k')$ ;
 \item the affine Springer fibers $\calM_{H,v}(a_H)\otimes_k k'$ and
   $\calM_{H,v}(a'_H)$ equipped with the action of
   $\calP_{v}(J_{H,a_H})\otimes_k k'$ and $\calP_v(J_{H,a'_H})$
   respectively, are isomorphic;
 \item the affine Springer fibers $\calM_v(a)\otimes_k k'$ and
   $\calM_v(a')$ equipped with the action of $\calP_v(J_a)$ and
   $\calP_v(J_{a'})$ respectively, are isomorphic.
\end{itemize}
Let $\delta_H(a_H)$ denote the dimension of the affine Springer fiber
$\calM_{H,v}(a_H)$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
There exists a geometrically connected smooth projective curve $X$ over
$k$ equipped with the following data:
\begin{itemize}
 \item two distinct $k$-points denoted $v$ and $\infty$,
 \item an isomorphism between the completion of $X$ at $v$ with the
   scheme $X_v$ above,
 \item a $\pi_0(\kappa)$-torsor $\rho_\kappa$ over $X$ equipped with a
   trivialization $\alpha_\infty$ above $\infty$ and an
   isomorphism $\alpha_v$ with $\rho_{\kappa,v}$ above
   $X_v$.
\end{itemize}
Let $G$ and $H$ be the associated $X$-group schemes as in
\ref{subsection : Groupes endoscopiques}. With the data of $\alpha_v$,
the restrictions of $G$ and $H$ to $X_v$ are canonically isomorphic
to $G_v$ and $H_v$. Since the centers of $G_v$ and $H_v$ do not contain
split tori, the same applies to $G$ and $H$. With the
data of $\alpha_\infty$, we have a homomorphism
$$\rho_\kappa^\bullet:\pi_1(X,\infty)=\pi_1(\ovl X,\infty)\rtimes \Gal(\bar k/ k)
\rightarrow \pi_0(\kappa)
$$
trivial on the factor $\Gal(\bar k/ k)$. Consequently, the subgroup
$\pi_1(\ovl X,\infty)$ has the same image in $\pi_0(\kappa)$ as
$\pi_1(X,\infty)$. It follows that over $\ovl X$, the centers of $G$ and $H$ do not
contain split tori.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
We now choose an invertible bundle $D$ on $X$ satisfying the
following hypotheses:
\begin{itemize}
 \item there exists an invertible bundle $D'$ on $X$ with $D={D'}^{\otimes
   2}$,
 \item a non-zero global section of $D'$ at $v$,
 \item $\deg(D) > rN+2g$ where $r$ is the rank of $G$ and $g$ is the genus
   of $X$,
 \item $\delta_H(a_H)$ is smaller than the integer $\delta_H^{\rm bad}(D)$
   defined in \ref{delta bad}.
\end{itemize}
According to \ref{codimension}, the integer $\delta_H^{\rm bad}$ tends to
$\infty$ as $\deg(D)\rightarrow \infty$, so that for $\deg(D)$
sufficiently large, the last hypothesis is satisfied.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Consider the Hitchin fibrations associated with curve $X$, the
invertible bundle $D$, and the groups $G$ and $H$ respectively
$$f:\calM\rightarrow \calA \ {\rm and}\ f_H:\calM_H\rightarrow \calA_H.$$
The hypothesis that the centers of $G$ and $H$ do not contain split tori
over $\ovl X$ and the hypothesis $\deg(D)>2g$ ensure that the
anisotropic open sets $\calA^\ani$ and $\calA_H^\ani$ are non-empty.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
With the hypothesis $\deg(D) > rN+2g$, the restriction map of
global sections to $\Spec(\calO_v/ \epsilon_v^N)$
$$\rmH^0(X,\frakc_{H,D}) \rightarrow \frakc_H(\calO_v/ \epsilon_v^N)$$
is a surjective linear map. Let $Z$ be the affine subspace of
$\rmH^0(X,\frakc_{H,D})$ of elements having the same image in
$\frakc_H(\calO_v/ \epsilon_v^N)$ as $a_H$. This is a closed sub-scheme
of codimension $rN$ of $\calA_H$. By the same reasoning
as in \ref{A diamond non vide}, we can show that the open set $Z'$ of
$Z$ consisting of points $a'_H\in Z'(\bar k)$ such that the curve $a'_H(\bar
X-\{v\})$ intersects the divisor $\discrim_{H,D}+\resultant_{H,D}^G$
transversally, is a non-empty open set. Since the complement of
$\calA_H^\ani$ in $\calA_H$ is a closed set of codimension greater
than or equal to $\deg(D)$ \cf \ref{codim complement de A ani}, $Z'\cap
\calA_H^\ani\not= \emptyset$. By replacing $Z'$ with $Z'\cap
\calA_H^\ani$, we can assume that $Z'\subset \calA_H^\ani$. For any
$a'_H\in Z'(\bar k)$, the invariant $\delta_H(a')=\delta_H(a_H)$ so that we
have $Z'\subset \calA_H^{\rm good}$ where we have the stabilization
theorem \cf \ref{subsection : A good}.

Let $\tilde Z'$ be the inverse image of $Z'$ in $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm
good}$. As $\tilde Z'$ is a scheme of positive dimension, there exists
an integer $m$ such that for any finite extension $k'/ k$ of degree greater than or
equal to $m$, the set $\tilde Z'(k')\not= \emptyset$. Let $\tilde a'_H
\in Z'(k')$ above $a'_H\in Z(k')$. Let $\tilde a'$ be the image of $\tilde
a'_H$ in $\tilde\calA$ and $a'$ be the image of $a'_H$ in $\calA$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
The conjunction of the part of the stabilization theorem \ref{stabilisation sur tilde
A} proved on the open set $\tilde\calA_H^{\rm good}$ with the
formula \ref{formule des points fixes Hitchin} gives us the equality
\begin{eqnarray*}
&{\sharp\,}(\calP'_{a'})^0(k') \prod_{v'\in |(X-U)\otimes_k k'|} {\sharp\,}
[\calM_{H,v'}(a'_H)/\calP_{v'}(J'_{a'})](k')_\kappa
\\
=&q^{r(a'_H)\deg(k'/ k)}{\sharp\,}
(\calP'_{a'})^0(k') \prod_{v'\in |(X-U)\otimes_k k'|}
{\sharp\,} [\calM_{v'}(a'_H)/\calP_{v'}(J'_{a'})](k')
\end{eqnarray*}
with a choice of a group scheme $J'_{a'}$ smooth, commutative, with connected
fiber, equipped with a homomorphism
$$J'_{a'}\rightarrow J_{a'}\rightarrow J_{H,a'_H}$$
as in \ref{choix J'_a}. It will be convenient to choose $J'_{a'}=J_{a'}^0$
above $X_v$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Since $\tilde a'_H\in \tilde\calU(k')$, we can apply \ref{LF simple}
to all places $v'\not=v$. By subtracting the equalities at these
places, we find an equality at the initial place $v$
$${\sharp\,}[\calM_{H,v}(a'_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_{a'})](k')_\kappa
=q^{r_v(a'_H)\deg(k'/ k)} {\sharp\,} [\calM_{v}(a'_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_{a'})](k').
$$
As we know that the affine Springer fiber $\calM_v(a)\otimes_k k'$ equipped
with the action of $\calP_v(J_a)\otimes_k k'$ is isomorphic to $\calM_v(a')$
equipped with the action of $\calP_v(J_{a'})$ and the same for group
$H$, we deduce the equality
$${\sharp\,}[\calM_{H,v}(a_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_a)](k')_\kappa
=q^{r_{H,v}^G(a_H)\deg(k'/ k)} {\sharp\,} [\calM_{v}(a_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_a)](k').
$$
for any extension $k'$ of $k$ of degree greater than $m$. By
applying \ref{k'->k}, we obtain the equality
$${\sharp\,}[\calM_{H,v}(a_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_a)](k)_\kappa
=q^{r_{H,v}^G(a_H)} {\sharp\,} [\calM_{v}(a_H)/\calP_{v}(J^0_a)](k).
$$
By applying formula \ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}, we now obtain
the equality
$${\bf O}_{a}^\kappa(1_{\frakg_v},{\rm d}t_v)=q^{r_{H,v}^G(a_H)}
{\bf SO}_{a_H}(1_{\frakh_v},{\rm d}t_v)
$$
which was desired.
\end{numero}

This completes the proof of the Langlands-Shelstad conjecture
\ref{LS}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Stabilization over $\tilde\calA^\ani$}
\label{subsection : renversement}\label{subsection : stabilisation geometrique}

By reversing the local-global process once again, we can
now complete the proof of \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}.

\begin{proof}
Let's keep the notations from \ref{subsection : A good}. As in
\ref{subsection : A good}, it suffices to prove the equality of traces
\ref{egalite de traces}
\begin{equation}\label{egalite de traces 2}
\sum_{n} (-1)^{n}\tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_\kappa)_{\tilde a}) \\
=\sum_{n} (-1)^{n} \tr(\sigma_{k'},(L^n_{H,{\rm st}})_{\tilde a_H}).
\end{equation}
for any $\tilde a_H\in\tilde\calA_H^\ani(k)$ with image $\tilde a\in
\tilde\calA^\ani(k)$. According to \ref{formule des points fixes Hitchin}, the
left side of \ref{egalite de traces} is
$$(\calP'_a)^0(k) \prod_{v\in |X-U|} \sharp\,
[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa
$$
while the right side is
$$q^{r_H^G(D)}(\calP'_a)^0(k) \prod_{v\in |X-U|} \sharp\,
[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k).
$$
By combining \ref{LS}, \ref{comptage avec P_v(J'_a)}, we have the equality
$$[\calM_v(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)_\kappa=q^{r_{H,v}^G(D)}[\calM_{H,v}(a)/\calP_v(J'_a)](k)$$
whence \ref{egalite de traces 2}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Waldspurger's Conjecture}
\label{subsection : demo Waldspurger}

Now let $G_1$ and $G_2$ be two $X$-group schemes
paired in the sense of \ref{apparies}. Suppose that the centers of $G_1$ and
$G_2$ do not contain split tori over $\ovl
X=X\otimes_k \bar k$.

\begin{numero}
Let $f_1:\calM_1 \rightarrow \calA_1$ and $f_2:\calM_2 \rightarrow
\calA_2$ be the associated Hitchin fibrations. As in \ref{Hitchin
apparies}, we have
$$\calA=\calA_1=\calA_2.$$
Let $\calP_1$ and $\calP_2$ be the $\calA$-Picard stacks associated with
$G_1$ and $G_2$. According to \ref{isogenie P1 P2}, there exists a
homomorphism $\calP_1\rightarrow \calP_2$ which induces an isogeny
between their neutral components.
\end{numero}

\begin{theoreme}\label{pair stable}
There exists an isomorphism between the simplifications of the perverse
graded sheaves on $\calA^\ani$
$$K_1=\bigoplus_n\,^p\rmH^n (f_{1,*}^\ani\Ql)_{\rm st} \ {\rm and}\
K_2=\bigoplus_n\,^p\rmH^n (f_{2,*}^\ani\Ql)_{\rm st}.$$
\end{theoreme}

The proof of this theorem essentially follows the same
steps as that of \ref{stabilisation sur tilde A}. In particular, we
will prove along the way the non-standard fundamental lemma
conjectured by Waldspurger \ref{Waldspurger}.

\begin{numero}
We first show that such an isomorphism exists above the open set
$\calA^\diamondsuit$. Above this open set, the morphisms $f_1$ and
$f_2$ are proper and smooth, so that the restrictions of $K_1$ and $K_2$
to $\calA^\diamondsuit$ are graded local systems. To
show that there exists an isomorphism between their simplifications, it
suffices, according to Chebotarev's theorem, to prove the equality of
traces
\begin{equation}\label{egalite des traces 3}
\tr(\sigma_{k'},K_{1,a})=\tr(\sigma_{k'},K_{2,a})
\end{equation}
for any finite extension $k'$ of $k$ and for any point $a\in
\calA^\diamondsuit(k')$. By replacing $X$ with $X\otimes_k k'$, we can
assume that $k=k'$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Let $a\in\calA^\diamondsuit(k)$. According to \ref{A lozenge}, we know:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\calP_{i,a}$ acts simply transitively on $\calM_{i,a}$
 \item $\calP_{i,a}$ is of the form $[P_i/ A_i]$ where $P_i$ is an
   extension of a finite group by an abelian variety and where
   $A_i$ is a finite group acting trivially on $P_i$.
\end{itemize}
Consequently, the quotient $[\calM_{i,a}/ P_i]$ is isomorphic to the
classifying stack of the finite group $A_i$. According to the fixed-point formula
\ref{formule des points fixes} and \ref{exemple : classifiant fini}, we have
$$\tr(\sigma,K_{i,a})=\sharp P_i^0(k).$$
Since $P_1^0$ and $P_2^0$ are isogenous abelian varieties over
$k$, they have the same number of $k$-points, hence the equality of traces
\ref{egalite des traces 3}. Therefore, there exists an isomorphism between the
simplifications of the restrictions of $K_1$ and $K_2$ to
$\calA^\diamondsuit$.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
% [journal 8.8.6] concerns the STABLE parts K_1, K_2 and cites 7.8.3, not the kappa-part 7.8.5.
According to the support theorem \ref{support stable}, there exists an
isomorphism between the semi-simplifications of the restrictions of $K_1$ and $K_2$
to
$$\calA^{\rm good}=\calA^{\rm ani}-\calA^{\rm bad}.$$
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
Proceeding as in \ref{lemme fondamental}, we deduce the
non-standard fundamental lemma conjectured by Waldspurger
\ref{Waldspurger}.
\end{numero}

\begin{numero}
By reversing the local-global process again as in
\ref{subsection : renversement}, we deduce the equality of traces
\ref{egalite des traces 3} for any $a\in \calA^\ani(k')$ for any
finite extension $k'$ of $k$. We deduce Theorem \ref{pair stable}.
\end{numero}

% [journal 8.8.9] MISSING FROM THE arXiv VERSION. The journal closes chapter 8 with this
% remark on what the delta-regularity conjecture would yield.
\begin{numero}
If one grants the conjecture \ref{conjecture delta regularite}, then Theorem \ref{support general} implies that the stable part of the perverse cohomology sheaves of the Hitchin fibration is the intermediate extension of its restriction to the open subset $\calA^\diamondsuit$, where it consists of local systems.
\end{numero}

\section[Poincar\'e Duality and Dimension Counting]{Poincar\'{e} Duality and Dimension Counting according to Goresky and MacPherson}
\label{appendice GM}

Goresky and MacPherson observed that Poincar\'{e} duality imposes
a constraint on the codimension of support of simple perverse sheaves
present in the decomposition theorem. This observation played a crucial
role in the proof of the support theorem. It seems to us that it should
also have other applications. With their permission, we recall this
argument of dimension counting and Poincar\'{e} duality in a general
context and in the simplest possible form. In full generality, this
constraint is relatively weak, but in practice, it provides a valuable
starting point with the help of which one can bring into play other more
specific arguments.

Let $X$ and $Y$ be finite type schemes over a finite field $k=\FF_q$. Let
$f:X\rightarrow Y$ be a proper morphism. Suppose furthermore that $X$ is a
smooth $k$-scheme. Then, according to Deligne \cite{Weil2}, the direct image
$f_*\Ql$ is a pure complex. According to \cite{BBD}, it decomposes
geometrically as a direct sum of geometrically irreducible perverse
sheaves with shifts. There exists an isomorphism over $Y\otimes_k \bar k$
$$f_*\Ql=\bigoplus_{(K,n)} K[-n]^{m_{K,n}}$$
where the direct sum is extended over the set of equivalence classes of
pairs $(K,n)$ consisting of an irreducible perverse sheaf $K$ on $Y\otimes_k
\bar k$ and an integer $n$, and where
$$m_{K,n}:=\dim\Hom(K,\,^p\rmH^n(f_*\Ql))$$
is a natural integer, zero except for a finite number of pairs $(K,n)$. A
geometrically irreducible perverse sheaf $K$ is said to be {\em present} in $f_*\Ql$ if
there exists an integer $n$ such that $m_{K,n}\not=0$.

Poincar\'{e} duality implies a symmetry for these integers
$m_{K,n}$.

\begin{appproposition}\label{symetrie Lefschetz}
For any geometrically irreducible perverse sheaf $K$ on $Y$,
then we have
$$m_{K,n+\dim(X)}=m_{DK,-n+\dim(X)}$$
where $DK$ is the Verdier dual of $K$.
\end{appproposition}

Goresky and MacPherson observed that this symmetry imposes a
constraint on the codimension of the supports of the geometrically
irreducible perverse sheaves $K$ present in $f_*\Ql$.

\begin{apptheoreme}\label{inegalite de codim}
Let $X$ and $Y$ be finite type schemes over a field $k$, with $X$ smooth
over $k$. Let $f:X\rta Y$ be a proper morphism. Suppose that the morphism
$f$ is of relative dimension $d$. Let $K$ be an irreducible perverse sheaf
on $Y\otimes_k \bar k$ present in $f_*\Ql$. Let $Z$ be the
support of $K$. Then we have the inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)\leq d.$$
\end{apptheoreme}

\begin{proof}
Suppose on the contrary that ${\rm codim}(Z)>d$. According to Poincar\'{e}
duality and by exchanging $K$ and $DK$ which have the same support,
we can assume that there exists an integer $n$
$$n\geq\dim(X)$$
such that $m_{K,n}\not=0$. According to \cite{BBD}, there exists an open set $Z'$ of
$Z$, a local system $K'$ on $S$ such that $K=j_{! *} K' [\dim(Z)]$ where
$j$ is the inclusion of the open set $Z'$ in $Z$. Let $y$ be a geometric point
of $Z'$. The fiber of $K$ at $y$ is then a $\Ql$-vector space
placed in degree
$$-\dim(Z)=-\dim(Y)+{\rm codim}(Z).$$
The fiber of $K[-n]$ at $y$ being a direct factor $\rmR\Gamma(X_y,\Ql)$, this implies that
$$\rmH^{n-\dim(Y)+{\rm codim}(Z)}(X_y)\not=0.$$
However, we have the inequality
$$n-\dim(Y)+{\rm codim}(Z) > 2d$$
because $n\geq\dim(X)$ and ${\rm codim}(Z) >d$. This non-vanishing is
in contradiction with the hypothesis that the fiber $X_y$ is of dimension
less than or equal to $d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{apptheoreme}\label{inegalite stricte de codim}
Let us assume the hypotheses of the previous theorem.
Suppose in addition that the fibers of $f$ are geometrically
irreducible of dimension $d>0$. Let $K$ be an irreducible perverse sheaf
on $Y\otimes_k \bar k$ present in $f_*\Ql$. Let $Z$ be the
support of $K$. Then we have the strict inequality
$${\rm codim}(Z)<d.$$
\end{apptheoreme}

\begin{proof}
Under the hypothesis that the fibers of $f$ are irreducible, the cohomology
sheaf of maximal degree $2d$ is the local system
$$\rmH^{2d}(f_*\Ql)=\Ql(-d).$$

Consider the decomposition of $f_*\Ql$ on $Y\otimes_k \bar k$
$$f_*\Ql=\bigoplus_{(L,n)} L[-n]^{m_{L,n}}$$
where $L$ ranges over the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible
perverse sheaves on $Y\otimes_k \bar k$ and $n$ the set of
integers. If $m_{L,n}\not=0$, then we have
$\rmH^i(L[-n])=0$ for all $i> 2d$ and even $\rmH^{2d}(L[-n])=0$ if $L$ is not
isomorphic to $\Ql[\dim(Y)]$.

The same argument as in the previous theorem shows that
for any irreducible perverse sheaf $K$ on $Y\otimes_k \bar k$ which
is not isomorphic to $\Ql[-\dim(Y)]$, then the support $Z$ of $K$ must
satisfy the strict inequality ${\rm codim}(Z)<d$. Of course, if $K$ is
isomorphic to $\Ql[-\dim(Y)]$, its support is
$Y$ entirely and the inequality is trivially satisfied.
\end{proof}
